that the second and fourth lines of Condition (E2) are satisfied. It also implies that
Condition (E4) holds true.

Since the process is irreducible and aperiodic and
K
is finite, (
3.2
) is clearly satisfied for
n_{0}=1
and

m_{0}
large enough, so that Theorem
8.2
follows from Proposition
3.3
.
∎

9
Proof of Theorem
2.1

In all the proof, the constants
C
are all positive and finite and may change from line to line. We first assume from
Subsections
9.1
to
9.6
that for all
n\geq 0
and all
x\in E
,
\mathbb{P}_{x}(n<\tau_{\partial})>0
. The general
case will be handled in Subsection
9.8
.

9.1
Main steps of the proof

The proof is based on a careful study of the semigroup of the process conditioned to not be absorbed before time
T
. In this
section, we give the main ideas and steps of the proof of (
2.1
) for the total variation norm

\|\cdot\|_{TV}:=\|\cdot\|_{TV(1)}
in place of
\|\cdot\|_{TV(\varphi_{1})}
, and leave the details for the following subsections,
where preliminary results and the following
Propositions
9.1
,
9.2
,
9.3
and
Lemma
9.4
are proved. The general case of the
\|\cdot\|_{TV(\varphi_{1})}
norm is handled in
Subsection
9.7
.

For any
T\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, we consider the law of the process
X
conditioned to not be absorbed before time
T
. We introduce the linear operators
(S_{m,n}^{T})_{0\leq m\leq n\leq T}
defined by

\displaystyle S_{m,n}^{T}f(x)=\mathbb{E}(f(X_{n})\mid X_{m}=x,\ T<\tau_{\partial})=\frac{P_{n-m}\left(fP_{T-n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}\right)(x)}{P_{T-m}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(x)}.

It is well-known that
(S_{m,n}^{T})_{0\leq m\leq n\leq T}
forms a time-inhomogeneous semigroup (i.e.
S_{m,n}^{T}S_{n,p}^{T}=S_{m,p}^{T}
for all
m\leq n\leq p\leq T
) and that the process
(X_{n},0\leq n\leq T)
under
\mathbb{P}^{S^{T}_{0,\cdot}}_{x}
is a (time-inhomogeneous) Markov process, where we
denote by
\mathbb{P}^{S^{T}_{0,\cdot}}_{x}
the law of the process
(X_{n},0\leq n\leq T)
conditionally on
T<\tau_{\partial}
and
X_{0}=x
.

Fix
\theta\in(\theta_{1}/\theta_{2},1)
. For any
T\geq 0
, we set, for
x\in E
,

\displaystyle\psi_{T}(x)=\mathbb{E}_{x}(\theta^{-T_{K}\wedge T}\mid T<\tau_{\partial})=\mathbb{E}_{x}^{S^{T}_{0,\cdot}}\left(\theta^{-T_{K}\wedge T}\right),

where

\displaystyle T_{K}:=\inf\{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}:X_{n}\in K\}

is the first hitting time of
K
by the process
(X_{n},n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+})
. Be careful that
T_{K}
is not the first hitting time of
K
by
the full process
(X_{t},t\in I)
, unless
I=\mathbb{Z}_{+}
.

The following proposition provides a Lyapunov-type property for the inhomogeneous semigroup
S
.

Proposition 9.1
.

There exists a constant
\bar{C}>0
such that, for all
0\leq m<T
and
1\leq k\leq T-m
,

\displaystyle S^{T}_{m,m+k}\psi_{T-(m+k)}(x)\leq\theta^{k}\psi_{T-m}(x)+\bar{C},\quad\forall x\in E.

(9.1)

The next proposition provides a Dobrushin coefficient-type property for the inhomogeneous semigroup
S
.

Proposition 9.2
.

There exists a constant
\alpha_{0}\in(0,1)
such that, for all
R>0
, there exists
k_{R}\geq 1
such that,
for all
T\geq k_{R}
and all
x,y\in E
such that

\psi_{T}(x)+\psi_{T}(y)\leq R
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\delta_{x}S_{0,k_{R}}^{T}-\delta_{y}S_{0,k_{R}}^{T}\right\|_{TV}\leq 2(1-\alpha_{0}).

The following property is a consequence of the two previous ones.

Proposition 9.3
.

There exist constants
n_{0}\geq 1
,
C>0
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
such that,
\forall n\geq 1
and all
x,y\in E
,

\displaystyle\left\|\delta_{x}S_{0,n_{0}n}^{n_{0}n}-\delta_{y}S_{0,n_{0}n}^{n_{0}n}\right\|_{TV}\leq C\alpha^{n}(2+\psi_{n_{0}n}(x)+\psi_{n_{0}n}(y)).

Let us now deduce (
2.1
) with the total variation norm in place of
\|\cdot\|_{TV(\varphi_{1})}
, from the last
proposition. We have, for all
x,y\in E
,

\left\|\delta_{x}P_{nn_{0}}-\delta_{x}P_{nn_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{E}\,\delta_{y}S_{0,n_{0}n}^{n_{0}n}\right\|_{TV}\\
\leq C\alpha^{n}\left(2\delta_{x}P_{nn_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{E}+\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\theta^{-T_{K}\wedge nn_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{nn_{0}<\tau_{\partial}}\right)+\psi_{n_{0}n}(y)\delta_{x}P_{nn_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{E}\right).

Hence, for any probability measure
\mu
on
E
, integrating the above inequality over
\mu(\mathrm{d}x)
leads to

\left\|\mu P_{nn_{0}}-\mu P_{nn_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{E}\,\delta_{y}S_{0,n_{0}n}^{n_{0}n}\right\|_{TV}\\
\leq C\alpha^{n}\left(2\mu P_{nn_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{E}+\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left(\theta^{-T_{K}\wedge nn_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{nn_{0}<\tau_{\partial}}\right)+\psi_{n_{0}n}(y)\mu P_{nn_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{E}\right).

We make use of the following lemma.

Lemma 9.4
.

For all
\theta\in(\theta_{1}/\theta_{2},1)
, there exists a constant
C
such that, for
all
0\leq m\leq T
and all probability measure
\mu
over
E
such that
\mu(\varphi_{2})>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left(\theta^{-T_{K}\wedge T}\mathbbm{1}_{T<\tau_{\partial}}\right)\leq C\,\frac{\mu(\varphi_{1})}{\mu(\varphi_{2})}\mathbb{P}_{\mu}\left(T<\tau_{\partial}\right).

This implies that, for all
\mu
such that
\mu(\varphi_{2})>0
,

\left\|\mu P_{nn_{0}}-\delta_{y}S_{0,n_{0}n}^{n_{0}n}\mu P_{nn_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{E}\right\|_{TV}\\
\leq C\alpha^{n}\left(2\mu P_{nn_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{E}+\frac{\mu(\varphi_{1})}{\mu(\varphi_{2})}\mu P_{nn_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{E}+\psi_{n_{0}n}(y)\mu P_{nn_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{E}\right).

Hence

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\mu P_{nn_{0}}}{\mu P_{nn_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}-\delta_{y}S_{0,n_{0}n}^{n_{0}n}\right\|_{TV}

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha^{n}\left(2+\frac{\mu(\varphi_{1})}{\mu(\varphi_{2})}+\psi_{n_{0}n}(y)\right).

Using the same procedure w.r.t.
y
, we deduce that, for any probability measures
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}
on
E
such that

\mu_{1}(\varphi_{2})>0
and
\mu_{2}(\varphi_{2})>0
,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\mu_{1}P_{nn_{0}}}{\mu_{1}P_{nn_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}-\frac{\mu_{2}P_{nn_{0}}}{\mu_{2}P_{nn_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}\right\|_{TV}

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha^{n}\left(\frac{\mu_{1}(\varphi_{1})}{\mu_{1}(\varphi_{2})}+\frac{\mu_{2}(\varphi_{1})}{\mu_{2}(\varphi_{2})}\right),

where we used the fact that
\mu(\varphi_{1})/\mu(\varphi_{2})\geq 1
for all probability measure
\mu
on
E
such that
\mu(\varphi_{2})>0
.

Because of Lemma
9.6
below, we deduce that, for some constant
D_{1}>0
and for all
0\leq k<n_{0}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\mu_{1}P_{nn_{0}+k}}{\mu_{1}P_{nn_{0}+k}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}-\frac{\mu_{2}P_{nn_{0}+k}}{\mu_{2}P_{nn_{0}+k}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}\right\|_{TV}

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha^{n}\left(\frac{\mu_{1}P_{k}\varphi_{1}}{\mu_{1}P_{k}\varphi_{2}}+\frac{\mu_{2}P_{k}\varphi_{1}}{\mu_{2}P_{k}\varphi_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha^{n}\left(\frac{\mu_{1}(\varphi_{1})}{\mu_{1}(\varphi_{2})}\vee D_{1}+\frac{\mu_{2}(\varphi_{1})}{\mu_{2}(\varphi_{2})}\vee D_{1}\right).

Therefore, up to a change in the constant
C
and replacing
\alpha
by
\alpha^{1/n_{0}}
, we deduce that, for all probability
measures
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}
on
E
such that
\mu_{1}(\varphi_{2})>0
and
\mu_{2}(\varphi_{2})>0
and for all
n\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\mu_{1}P_{n}}{\mu_{1}P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}-\frac{\mu_{2}P_{n}}{\mu_{2}P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}\right\|_{TV}

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha^{n}\left(\frac{\mu_{1}(\varphi_{1})}{\mu_{1}(\varphi_{2})}+\frac{\mu_{2}(\varphi_{1})}{\mu_{2}(\varphi_{2})}\right).

(9.2)

Fix
x_{0}\in K
. We set
\mu_{1}=\delta_{x_{0}}
and
\mu_{2}=\frac{\mu_{1}P_{1}}{\mu_{1}P_{1}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}
in (
9.2
). Since

\frac{\mu_{1}\varphi_{1}}{\mu_{1}\varphi_{2}}<\infty
and because of Lemma
9.6
below, we have

\frac{\mu_{2}\varphi_{1}}{\mu_{2}\varphi_{2}}<\infty
. We deduce that, for some constant
C>0
,

\left\|\frac{\delta_{x_{0}}P_{n+1}}{\delta_{x_{0}}P_{n+1}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}-\frac{\delta_{x_{0}}P_{n}}{\delta_{x_{0}}P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}\right\|_{TV}\leq C\alpha^{n},

and hence, using the completeness of the space of probability measures on
E
for the total variation norm, we deduce that there exists a quasi-limiting measure
\nu_{QSD}
(which is hence a quasi-stationary
distribution) such that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\delta_{x_{0}}P_{n}}{\delta_{x_{0}}P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}-\nu_{QSD}\right\|_{TV}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2C}{1-\alpha}\alpha^{n}.

In particular, it follows from Lemma
9.8
below that
\nu_{QSD}(K)>0
and hence that
\nu_{QSD}(\varphi_{2})>0
. Since
Lemma
9.6
implies that
\frac{P_{n}\varphi_{1}(x_{0})}{P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(x_{0})}
is uniformly bounded in
n\geq 0
, we
deduce that
\nu_{QSD}(\varphi_{1}\wedge M)
is bounded uniformly in
M>0
and hence
\nu_{QSD}(\varphi_{1})<\infty
.

Using (
9.2
) again (up to another change of the constant
C
), we obtain that, for all probability measure
\mu
on
E

such that
\frac{\mu(\varphi_{1})}{\mu(\varphi_{2})}<\infty
,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\mu P_{n}}{\mu P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}-\nu_{QSD}\right\|_{TV}

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha^{n}\frac{\mu(\varphi_{1})}{\mu(\varphi_{2})}.

This also entails that there exists a unique quasi-stationary distribution such that

\nu_{QSD}(\varphi_{1})/\nu_{QSD}(\varphi_{2})<\infty
.

This ends the proof of (
2.1
) for the total variation norm. The general case with the norm

\|\cdot\|_{TV(\varphi_{1})}
is proved in Subsection
9.7
.

9.2
Preliminary results

We start by proving two basic inequalities which are direct consequences of (E2).

Lemma 9.5
.

For all
x\in E\setminus K
and all
n\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}_{x}(n<T_{K}\wedge\tau_{\partial})\leq\mathbb{E}_{x}[\varphi_{1}(X_{n})\mathbbm{1}_{n<T_{K}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}]\leq\theta_{1}^{n}\varphi_{1}(x).

For all
x\in E
and
n\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}_{x}(n<\tau_{\partial})\geq\mathbb{E}_{x}[\varphi_{2}(X_{n})\mathbbm{1}_{n<\tau_{\partial}}]\geq\theta_{2}^{n}\varphi_{2}(x).

Proof of Lemma
9.5
.

These two properties follow easily by induction from (E2). For example, the first one makes use of the following relation: for all

n\geq 1
and
x\in E
,

\mathbb{E}_{x}[\varphi_{1}(X_{n})\mathbbm{1}_{n<T_{K}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}]=\mathbbm{1}_{x\in E\setminus K}\,P_{1}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\left(\varphi_{1}(X_{n-1})\mathbbm{1}_{n-1<T_{K}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}\right)\right](x).

This and (E2) entail the property at time
n=1
and, by induction, at any time
n\geq 1
.
∎

The next lemma states that the expectation of
\varphi_{1}(X_{n})
is controlled by the expectation of
\varphi_{2}(X_{n})
uniformly in
time.

Lemma 9.6
.

For all
\theta\in(\theta_{1}/\theta_{2},1]
, there exists a finite constant
D_{\theta}>0
such that, for all probability measure
\mu

on
E
such that
\mu(\varphi_{1})/\mu(\varphi_{2})<\infty
, for all
T\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and all
x\in E
,

\frac{\mu P_{T}\varphi_{1}}{\mu P_{T}\varphi_{2}}\leq\left(\theta^{T}\frac{\mu(\varphi_{1})}{\mu(\varphi_{2})}\right)\vee D_{\theta}.

(9.3)

Proof of Lemma
9.6
.

It follows from (E2) that

\mu P_{T+1}\varphi_{1}\leq\theta_{1}\mu P_{T}\varphi_{1}+C\mu P_{T}\mathbbm{1}_{K}

and

\mu P_{T+1}\varphi_{2}\geq\theta_{2}\mu P_{T}\varphi_{2}.

Hence

\displaystyle\frac{\mu P_{T+1}\varphi_{1}}{\mu P_{T+1}\varphi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\theta_{1}\mu P_{T}\varphi_{1}+C\mu P_{T}\mathbbm{1}_{K}(x)}{\theta_{2}\mu P_{T}\varphi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\theta_{1}}{\theta_{2}}\,\frac{\mu P_{T}\varphi_{1}}{\mu P_{T}\varphi_{2}}+\frac{C}{\theta_{2}\inf_{y\in K}\varphi_{2}(y)}.

Since
\theta_{1}/\theta_{2}<\theta
, these arithmetico-geometric inequalities entail (
9.3
).
∎

We now give an irreducibility inequality.

Lemma 9.7
.

For all
C\geq 1
, there exists a time
n_{5}(C)\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle a_{5}(C):=\inf_{\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{1}(E)\text{ s.t.\ }\mu(\varphi_{1})\leq C\mu(\varphi_{2})}\mathbb{P}_{\mu}(X_{n_{5}(C)}\in K)>0.

(9.4)

Proof of Lemma
9.7
.

It follows from (E4) that there exists a time
n_{\nu}\in\mathbb{N}
such that, for all
n\geq n_{\nu}
,

\mathbb{P}_{\nu}(X_{n}\in K)>0
, and, using (E1), that for all
n\geq n_{\nu}+n_{1}
,

\inf_{x\in K}\mathbb{P}_{x}(X_{n}\in K)\geq c_{1}\mathbb{P}_{\nu}(X_{n-n_{1}}\in K)>0.

Let
C\geq 1
and
\mu
be such that
\mu(\varphi_{1})\leq C\mu(\varphi_{2})
. It follows from Lemma
9.5
that,
for all
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mu}(T_{K}\wedge\tau_{\partial}>n)\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left[\varphi_{1}(X_{n})\mathbbm{1}_{T_{K}\wedge\tau_{\partial}>n}\right]\leq\theta_{1}^{n}\mu(\varphi_{1})\leq C\theta_{1}^{n}\mu(\varphi_{2}).

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mu}(n<\tau_{\partial})\geq\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[\varphi_{2}(X_{n})]\geq\theta_{2}^{n}\mu(\varphi_{2}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mu}(T_{K}\leq n<\tau_{\partial})\geq\left(\theta_{2}^{n}-C\theta_{1}^{n}\right)\mu(\varphi_{2}).

Choosing
n(C)=\lceil 2C/\log(\theta_{2}/\theta_{1})\rceil
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mu}(T_{K}\leq n(C)<\tau_{\partial})\geq\frac{\theta_{2}^{n(C)}}{2}\mu(\varphi_{2})\geq\frac{\theta_{2}^{n(C)}}{2C}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mu}(X_{n(C)+n_{\nu}+n_{1}}\in K)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{T_{K}\leq n(C)}\mathchoice{{\mathbb{P}_{X_{T_{K}}}(X_{n(C)+n_{\nu}+n_{1}-k}\in K)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=3.57002pt}}_{\,k=T_{K}}}{{\mathbb{P}_{X_{T_{K}}}(X_{n(C)+n_{\nu}+n_{1}-k}\in K)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=3.57002pt}}_{\,k=T_{K}}}{{\mathbb{P}_{X_{T_{K}}}(X_{n(C)+n_{\nu}+n_{1}-k}\in K)\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=3.73529pt}}_{\,k=T_{K}}}{{\mathbb{P}_{X_{T_{K}}}(X_{n(C)+n_{\nu}+n_{1}-k}\in K)\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=4.32085pt}}_{\,k=T_{K}}}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\min_{n_{\nu}+n_{1}\leq k\leq n_{\nu}+n_{1}+n(C)}\,\inf_{x\in K}\mathbb{P}_{x}(X_{k}\in K)\,\frac{\theta_{2}^{n(C)}}{2C}.

Hence we have proved Lemma
9.7
with
n_{5}(C)=n_{\nu}+n_{1}+n(C)
.
∎

The next lemma shows that conditional distributions with initial conditions in
K
give to
K
a mass uniformly bounded from below.

Lemma 9.8
.

There exists a time
n_{6}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle\inf_{T\geq n_{6}}\inf_{x\in K}\mathbb{P}_{x}(X_{T}\in K\mid T<\tau_{\partial})>0.

Proof of Lemma
9.8
.

Since
\varphi_{1}/\varphi_{2}
is bounded over
K
, we deduce from Lemma
9.6
that, setting
C:=D_{1}+\sup_{x\in K}\frac{\varphi_{1}(x)}{\varphi_{2}(x)}
, we have for all
x\in K
and all
T\geq n_{5}(C)
,

\displaystyle\frac{P_{T-n_{5}(C)}\varphi_{1}(x)}{P_{T-n_{5}(C)}\varphi_{2}(x)}

\displaystyle\leq C.

(9.5)

Using Lemma
9.7
applied to
\mu=\frac{\delta_{x}P_{T-n_{5}(C)}}{\delta_{x}P_{T-n_{5}(C)}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}
, we deduce that, for all
x\in K
and

T\geq n_{5}(C)
,

\mathbb{P}_{x}(X_{T}\in K\mid T<\tau_{\partial})=\frac{\mu P_{n_{5}(C)}\mathbbm{1}_{K}}{\mu P_{n_{5}(C)}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}\geq\mu P_{n_{5}(C)}\mathbbm{1}_{K}\geq a_{5}(C).\qed

The next lemma shows that survival probabilities are controlled by the function
\varphi_{1}
.

Lemma 9.9
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that, for all
p\in[1,\log\theta_{1}/\log\theta_{2})
,
x\in E
and
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}(n<\tau_{\partial})\leq C\,\frac{\varphi_{1}(x)^{1/p}}{1-\theta_{1}^{1/p}/\theta_{2}}\,\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(n<\tau_{\partial}).

(9.6)

Proof of Lemma
9.9
.

It follows from Lemma
9.5
that, for all
p\geq 1
,
x\in E\setminus K
and
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}(n<T_{K}\wedge\tau_{\partial})\leq\theta_{1}^{n/p}\varphi_{1}(x)^{1/p}.

(9.7)

Note that this inequality is trivial for
x\in K
. In particular, for
p\geq 1
such that
\theta_{1}^{1/p}<\theta_{2}
, for all
x\in K
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x}(\theta_{2}^{-T_{K}\wedge\tau_{\partial}})\leq\frac{\varphi_{1}(x)^{1/p}}{1-\theta_{1}^{1/p}/\theta_{2}}.

(9.8)

Fix
p\in[1,\log\theta_{1}/\log\theta_{2})
. Using (
9.7
), the second inequality
of Lemma
9.5
and (E3), we have for all
x\in E

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}(n<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{x}(n<T_{K}\wedge\tau_{\partial})+\mathbb{P}_{x}(T_{K}\wedge\tau_{\partial}\leq n<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\leq\theta_{2}^{n}\varphi_{1}(x)^{1/p}+\sum_{k=0}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{x}(T_{K}\wedge\tau_{\partial}=k)\sup_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(n-k<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\inf_{z\in K}\mathbb{P}_{z}(n<\tau_{\partial})}{\inf_{z\in K}\varphi_{2}(z)}\,\varphi_{1}(x)^{1/p}+c_{3}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{x}(T_{K}\wedge\tau_{\partial}=k)\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(n-k<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\leq C\inf_{z\in K}\mathbb{P}_{z}(n<\tau_{\partial})\,\varphi_{1}(x)^{1/p}+C\inf_{z\in K}\mathbb{P}_{z}(n<\tau_{\partial})\sum_{k=0}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{x}(T_{K}\wedge\tau_{\partial}=k)\theta_{2}^{-k},

(9.9)

where we used the fact that, for some constant
C>0
, for all
n\geq k\geq 0
and all
z\in K
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{z}(n<\tau_{\partial})\geq C\theta_{2}^{k}\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(n-k<\tau_{\partial}).

(9.10)

This is proved using the three following equations. For all
n\geq k\geq n_{6}
and all
z\in K
, by Lemmata
9.8

and
9.5
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{z}(n<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}_{z}(X_{k}\in K\mid k<\tau_{\partial})\mathbb{P}_{z}(k<\tau_{\partial})\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(n-k<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\geq C\theta_{2}^{k}\varphi_{2}(z)\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(n-k<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\geq C\theta_{2}^{k}\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(n-k<\tau_{\partial}),

since
\inf_{z\in K}\varphi_{2}(z)>0
. Also, for all
n\geq n_{6}\geq k
, using the last inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{z}(n<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\geq C\theta_{2}^{n_{6}}\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(n-n_{6}<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\geq C\theta_{2}^{n_{6}}\,\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(n-k<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\geq(C\theta_{2}^{n_{6}})\,\theta_{2}^{k}\,\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(n-k<\tau_{\partial}).

Finally, for all
k\leq n<n_{6}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{z}(n<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}_{z}(n_{6}<\tau_{\partial})\geq C\theta_{2}^{n_{6}}\geq(C\theta_{2}^{n_{6}})\,\theta_{2}^{k}\,\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(n-k<\tau_{\partial}),

so (
9.10
) is proved.

Combining (
9.8
) and (
9.9
) ends the proof of Lemma
9.9
.
∎

9.3
Proof of Proposition
9.1

Markov’s property implies that, for all
x\in E\setminus K
and
T,m\geq 1
,

\displaystyle S^{T}_{0,1}\psi_{T-1}(x)

\displaystyle=S^{T+m}_{m,m+1}\psi_{T-1}(x)=\theta\psi_{T}(x).

(9.11)

Indeed,

\displaystyle\theta\psi_{T}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{x}(\theta^{1-T_{K}\wedge T}\mathbbm{1}_{T<\tau_{\partial}})}{\mathbb{P}_{x}(T<\tau_{\partial})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{1<\tau_{\partial}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}}(\theta^{-T_{K}\wedge(T-1)}\mid T-1<\tau_{\partial})\mathbb{P}_{X_{1}}(T-1<\tau_{\partial})\right]}{\mathbb{P}_{x}(T<\tau_{\partial})}=S^{T}_{0,1}\psi_{T-1}(x).

Similarly, for all
x\in
K,

\displaystyle S^{T}_{0,1}\psi_{T-1}(x)

\displaystyle=S^{T+m}_{m,m+1}\psi_{T-1}(x)=\theta\mathbb{E}^{S^{T}_{0,\cdot}}_{x}(\theta^{-\sigma_{K}\wedge T}),

(9.12)

where

\displaystyle\sigma_{K}:=\min\{n\geq 1,\ X_{n}\in K\}

is the first return time in
K
. Setting

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle:=\sup_{T\geq 0}\sup_{x\in K}\mathbb{E}^{S_{0,\cdot}^{T}}_{x}(\theta^{-\sigma_{K}\wedge T}),

which is finite (see Lemma
9.10
), we can apply recursively (
9.11
)
and (
9.12
) to obtain

\displaystyle S_{m,m+k}^{T}\psi_{T-(m+k)}

\displaystyle=S_{m,m+k-1}^{T}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{E\setminus K}S_{m+k-1,m+k}^{T}(\psi_{T-(m+k)})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+S_{m,m+k-1}^{T}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{K}S_{m+k-1,m+k}^{T}(\psi_{T-(m+k)})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\theta S_{m,m+k-1}^{T}\psi_{T-(m+k-1)}+C\theta

\displaystyle\leq\ldots\leq\theta^{k}\psi_{T-m}(x)+C\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\theta^{\ell}.

Hence Proposition
9.1
follows from the next lemma.

Lemma 9.10
.

For all
\theta\in(\theta_{1}/\theta_{2},1)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{T\geq 0}\sup_{x\in K}\mathbb{E}^{S_{0,\cdot}^{T}}_{x}(\theta^{-\sigma_{K}\wedge T})<\infty.

Proof of Lemma
9.10
.

Fix
x\in K
. On the one hand, by Lemma
9.9
(with
p=1
), we have for any
1\leq n<T
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}(n<\sigma_{K}\text{ and }T<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{x}(\mathbbm{1}_{n<\sigma_{K}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}\mathbb{P}_{X_{n}}(T-n<\tau_{\partial}))

\displaystyle\leq C\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(T-n<\tau_{\partial})\mathbb{E}_{x}(\mathbbm{1}_{n<\sigma_{K}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}\varphi_{1}(X_{n})).

Using (E2) and Markov’s property as in the proof of Lemma
9.5
, we deduce

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}(n<\sigma_{K}\text{ and }T<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\leq C\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(T-n<\tau_{\partial})\theta_{1}^{n-1}P_{1}\varphi_{1}(x)

(9.13)

\displaystyle\leq C\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(T-n<\tau_{\partial})\theta_{1}^{n}.

(9.14)

On the other hand, Lemma
9.8
implies the existence of a constant
C>0
such that, for all
x\in K
and all
n\geq n_{6}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}(X_{n}\in K)\geq C\mathbb{P}_{x}(n<\tau_{\partial}).

We deduce from Markov’s property and Lemma
9.5
that, for all
T\geq n\geq n_{6}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}(T<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}_{x}(X_{n}\in K)\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(T-n<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\geq C\mathbb{P}_{x}(n<\tau_{\partial})\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(T-n<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\geq C\theta_{2}^{n}\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(T-n<\tau_{\partial}).

Combining this with (
9.13
), we finally deduce that there exists a
constant
C>0
such that, for all
x\in K
and all
T\geq n\geq n_{6}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}(n<\sigma_{K}\mid T<\tau_{\partial})\leq C\left(\frac{\theta_{1}}{\theta_{2}}\right)^{n}.

(9.15)

The extension to any
T\geq n
is trivial, so the conclusion follows.
∎

9.4
Proof of Proposition
9.2

We start by stating a lemma
proved at the end of this subsection.

Lemma 9.11
.

For all
x\in K
and
n_{1}+n_{6}\leq n\leq T
,

\mathbb{P}_{x}(X_{n}\in\cdot\mid T<\tau_{\partial})\geq c^{\prime}_{1}\nu,

(9.16)

where the measure
\nu
and the integer
n_{1}
are the one of Condition (E1), the integer
n_{6}
is from Lemma
9.7
and

c^{\prime}_{1}>0
is independent of
x,n
and
T
.

Fix
\theta\in(\theta_{1}/\theta_{2},1)
and set
k_{R}=\lceil\log(2R)/\log(1/\theta)\rceil+n_{1}+n_{6}
and fix
T\geq k_{R}
. For all
x\in E
such that
\psi_{T}(x)\leq R
, Markov’s inequality
implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}(T_{K}>k_{R}-n_{1}-n_{6}\mid T<\tau_{\partial})=\mathbb{P}^{S_{0,\cdot}^{T}}_{x}\left(T_{K}>k_{R}-n_{1}-n_{6}\right)\leq\frac{R}{\theta^{-k_{R}+n_{1}+n_{6}}}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

It follows from Lemma
9.11
that, for all measurable
A\subset E
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{S_{0,\cdot}^{T}}_{x}\left(X_{k_{R}}\in A\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{k_{R}-n_{1}-n_{6}}\mathbbm{1}_{T_{K}=k}\mathbb{P}_{X_{k}}(X_{k_{R}-k}\in A,\,T-k<\tau_{\partial})\right]}{\mathbb{P}_{x}(T<\tau_{\partial})}

\displaystyle\geq c^{\prime}_{1}\nu(A)\frac{\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{k_{R}-n_{1}-n_{6}}\mathbbm{1}_{T_{K}=k}\mathbb{P}_{X_{k}}(T-k<\tau_{\partial})\right]}{\mathbb{P}_{x}(T<\tau_{\partial})}

\displaystyle=c^{\prime}_{1}\nu(A)\mathbb{P}_{x}(T_{K}\leq k_{R}-n_{1}-n_{6}\mid T<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}c^{\prime}_{1}\nu(A).

This concludes the proof of Proposition
9.2
with
\alpha_{0}=c^{\prime}_{1}/2
.

Proof of Lemma
9.11
.

For all measurable set
A\subset K
, we deduce from Markov’s property that, for all
x\in K
and all
T\geq n\geq n_{1}+n_{6}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}(X_{n}\in A,\,T<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{X_{n-n_{1}}\in K}\,\mathbb{E}_{X_{n-n_{1}}}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{X_{n_{1}}\in A}\mathbb{P}_{X_{n_{1}}}(T-n<\tau_{\partial})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{X_{n-n_{1}}\in K}\,\mathbb{P}_{X_{n-n_{1}}}(X_{n_{1}}\in A)\right]\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(T-n<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\geq c_{1}\nu(A)\mathbb{P}_{x}\left(X_{n-n_{1}}\in K\right)\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(T-n<\tau_{\partial}),

(9.17)

where we used (E1). Now, using Lemma
9.9
, we deduce that there exists a constant
c>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}(T<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{x}(T-n_{1}<\tau_{\partial})=\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{n-n_{1}<\tau_{\partial}}\mathbb{P}_{X_{n-n_{1}}}(T-n<\tau_{\partial})\right)

\displaystyle\leq c\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{n-n_{1}<\tau_{\partial}}\varphi_{1}(X_{n-n_{1}})\right)\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(T-n<\tau_{\partial}).

Since
\varphi_{1}(x)/\varphi_{2}(x)
is uniformly bounded over
x\in K
, Lemma
9.6
implies that there
exists a constant
c^{\prime}>0
such that, for all
x\in K
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{n-n_{1}<\tau_{\partial}}\varphi_{1}(X_{n-n_{1}})\right]\leq c^{\prime}\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{n-n_{1}<\tau_{\partial}}\varphi_{2}(X_{n-n_{1}})\right]\leq c^{\prime}\mathbb{P}_{x}\left(n-n_{1}<\tau_{\partial}\right).

But
n-n_{1}\geq n_{6}
, hence Lemma
9.8
entails that there exists a constant
c^{\prime\prime}>0
such that, for all
x\in K
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\left(n-n_{1}<\tau_{\partial}\right)\leq c^{\prime\prime}\mathbb{P}_{x}(X_{n-n_{1}}\in K).

Hence we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}(T<\tau_{\partial})\leq cc^{\prime}c^{\prime\prime}\,\mathbb{P}_{x}\left(X_{n-n_{1}}\in K\right)\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(T-n<\tau_{\partial}).

Combining this with (
9.17
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}(X_{n}\in A\mid T<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{1}}{cc^{\prime}c^{\prime\prime}}\,\nu(A).

This ends the proof of Lemma
9.11
.
∎

9.5
Proof of Proposition
9.3

We transpose the ideas of
[
57
]
(see also
[
58
]
) to the time-inhomogeneous setting. We fix the
constants
R=4\bar{C}/(1-\theta)
and
\beta=\alpha_{0}/2\bar{C}
, where
\bar{C}
is the constant of
Proposition
9.1
. For all
T\geq 0
and all
\varphi:E\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, we set

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\varphi\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{T}=\sup_{x,y\in E}\frac{|\varphi(x)-\varphi(y)|}{2+\beta\psi_{T}(x)+\beta\psi_{T}(y)}.

Fix
n
and
T\geq 0
such that
(n+1)k_{R}\leq T
and let
\varphi
be such that
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\varphi\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{T-(n+1)k_{R}}\leq 1
. Then, replacing

\varphi
by
\varphi+c
for some appropriate constant
c
, one has
|\varphi|\leq 1+\beta\psi_{T-(n+1)k_{R}}
(see Lemma 3.8 p.14 in
[
57
]
).

If
\psi_{T-nk_{R}}(x)+\psi_{T-nk_{R}}(y)>R
, then, using Proposition
9.1
,

\left|S_{nk_{R},(n+1)k_{R}}^{T}\varphi(x)-S_{nk_{R},(n+1)k_{R}}^{T}\varphi(y)\right|\\
\begin{aligned} &\leq 2+\theta\beta\psi_{T-nk_{R}}(x)+\theta\beta\psi_{T-nk_{R}}(y)+2\beta\bar{C}\\
&\leq 2+\left(\theta+(1-\theta)/2\right)(\beta\psi_{T-nk_{R}}(x)+\beta\psi_{T-nk_{R}}(y))\\
&\quad\quad\quad-(R\beta)(1-\theta)/2+2\beta\bar{C}\\
&\leq(1-\alpha_{1})(2+\beta\psi_{T-nk_{R}}(x)+\beta\psi_{T-nk_{R}}(x)),\end{aligned}

where
\alpha_{1}\in(0,1)
is such that
2+\left(\theta+(1-\theta)/2\right)y\leq(1-\alpha_{1})(2+y)
for all
y\geq\beta R
.

If
\psi_{T-nk_{R}}(x)+\psi_{T-nk_{R}}(y)\leq R
, then, considering

\displaystyle\varphi=\varphi^{\prime}+\varphi^{\prime\prime},

with
|\varphi^{\prime}|\leq 1
and
|\varphi^{\prime\prime}|\leq\beta\psi_{T-(n+1)k_{R}}
, Propositions
9.1

and
9.2
entail

\left|S_{nk_{R},(n+1)k_{R}}^{T}\varphi(x)-S_{nk_{R},(n+1)k_{R}}^{T}\varphi(y)\right|\\
\leq 2(1-\alpha_{0})+\beta\theta\psi_{T-nk_{R}}(x)+\beta\theta\psi_{T-nk_{R}}(y)+2\beta\bar{C}.

Our choice
\beta=\alpha_{0}/2\bar{C}
implies that

\displaystyle\left|S_{nk_{R},(n+1)k_{R}}^{T}\varphi(x)-S_{nk_{R},(n+1)k_{R}}^{T}\varphi(y)\right|\leq(1-\alpha_{2})(2+\beta\psi_{T-nk_{R}}(x)+\beta\psi_{T-nk_{R}}(y)).

for the constant
\alpha_{2}=\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2}\wedge(1-\theta)>0
.

Hence, we obtained

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|S_{nk_{R},(n+1)k_{R}}^{T}\varphi\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{T-nk_{R}}\leq(1-\alpha_{1}\wedge\alpha_{2}){\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\varphi\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{T-(n+1)k_{R}},

which implies by iteration that

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|S_{0,nk_{R}}^{nk_{R}}\varphi\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{nk_{R}}\leq(1-\alpha_{1}\wedge\alpha_{2})^{n}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\varphi\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{0}\leq(1-\alpha_{1}\wedge\alpha_{2})^{n}\|\varphi\|_{\infty}/(1+\beta).

This concludes the proof of Proposition
9.3
.

9.6
Proof of Lemma
9.4

This lemma in a generalization of Lemma
9.10
. Its proof is based on similar computations. We give the details
for sake of completeness.

For all probability measure
\mu
on
E
, for any
0\leq n<T
, using Lemma
9.9
for the second inequality and
Lemma
9.5
for the third inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mu}(n<T_{K}\text{ and }T<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(\mathbbm{1}_{n<T_{K}}\mathbb{P}_{X_{n}}(T-n<\tau_{\partial}))

\displaystyle\leq C\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(T-n<\tau_{\partial})\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(\mathbbm{1}_{n<T_{K}}\varphi_{1}(X_{n}))

\displaystyle\leq C\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(T-n<\tau_{\partial})\theta_{1}^{n}\mu(\varphi_{1}).

(9.18)

For all integer
n\geq n_{\mu}
, where

\displaystyle n_{\mu}:=\left\lceil n_{5}(D_{\theta})+\frac{\log\frac{\mu(\varphi_{1})}{D_{\theta}\mu(\varphi_{2})}}{\log(1/\theta)}\right\rceil,

it follows from Lemma
9.6
that

\displaystyle\frac{\mu P_{n-n_{5}(D_{\theta})}\varphi_{1}}{\mu P_{n-n_{5}(D_{\theta})}\varphi_{2}}\leq D_{\theta}\vee\left(\theta^{n-n_{5}(D_{\theta})}\frac{\mu(\varphi_{1})}{\mu(\varphi_{2})}\right)\leq D_{\theta}

and from Lemma
9.7
that

\displaystyle\frac{\mu P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{K}}{\mu P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}\geq a_{5}(D_{\theta})>0.

Therefore, we obtain from the Markov property and Lemma
9.5
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mu}(T<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}_{\mu}(X_{n}\in K)\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(T-n<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\geq a_{5}(D_{\theta})\mathbb{P}_{\mu}(n<\tau_{\partial})\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(T-n<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\geq a_{5}(D_{\theta})\theta_{2}^{n}\mu(\varphi_{2})\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(T-n<\tau_{\partial}).

Combining this with (
9.18
), we obtain that, for all
n\geq n_{\mu}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mu}(n<T_{K}\text{ and }T<\tau_{\partial})\leq\frac{C}{a_{5}(D_{\theta})}\left(\frac{\theta_{1}}{\theta_{2}}\right)^{n}\frac{\mu(\varphi_{1})}{\mu(\varphi_{2})}\mathbb{P}_{\mu}(T<\tau_{\partial}).

Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left(\theta^{-T_{K}\wedge T}\mathbbm{1}_{T_{K}\geq n_{\mu},\ T<\tau_{\partial}}\right)\leq C\frac{\mu(\varphi_{1})}{\mu(\varphi_{2})}\mathbb{P}_{\mu}\left(T<\tau_{\partial}\right).

We deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left(\theta^{-T_{K}\wedge T}\mathbbm{1}_{T<\tau_{\partial}}\right)\leq\left(C\frac{\mu(\varphi_{1})}{\mu(\varphi_{2})}+\theta^{-n_{\mu}}\right)\mathbb{P}_{\mu}\left(T<\tau_{\partial}\right).

Since
\theta^{-n_{\mu}}\leq\frac{\theta^{-(n_{5}(D_{\theta})+1)}\mu(\varphi_{1})}{D_{\theta}\mu(\varphi_{2})}
, we have proved
Lemma
9.4
.

9.7
Conclusion of the proof of (
2.1
) for the norm
\|\cdot\|_{TV(\varphi_{1})}

For all
n\geq 1
, we introduce the linear operator on
L^{\infty}(\varphi_{1})
, defined for all
h\in L^{\infty}(\varphi_{1})
as

\displaystyle R_{n}h(x)=\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(h(X_{n})\mathbbm{1}_{T_{K}\leq n<\tau_{\partial}}\right),\quad\forall x\in E.

(9.19)

Note that this operator is well-defined since
|R_{n}h(x)|\leq\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(\varphi_{1})}\,P_{n}\varphi_{1}(x)<\infty
. We first give some properties of
R_{n}
,
which can be seen as a bounded approximation of
P_{n}
in
L^{\infty}(\varphi_{1})
.

Lemma 9.12
.

We have

\displaystyle\bar{R}:=\sup_{n\geq 1}\sup_{x\in E}R_{n}\varphi_{1}(x)<\infty,

and for all
n\geq 1
and
x\in E
,

\displaystyle 0\leq P_{n}\varphi_{1}(x)-R_{n}\varphi_{1}(x)\leq\theta_{1}^{n}\varphi_{1}(x).

Proof.

Using Markov’s property,

\displaystyle R_{n}\varphi_{1}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\leq n}\mathbb{E}_{x}[\mathbbm{1}_{T_{K}=k}P_{n-k}\varphi_{1}(X_{k})]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{y\in K,\ k\geq 0}P_{k}\varphi_{1}(y)\,\mathbb{P}_{x}(T_{K}\leq n)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{y\in K,\ k\geq 0}\frac{P_{k}\varphi_{1}(y)}{P_{k}\varphi_{2}(y)}\leq D_{1}\vee\sup_{y\in K}\frac{\varphi_{1}(y)}{\varphi_{2}(y)}<+\infty

by Lemma
9.6
. This proves the first inequality. For the second one, we observe that for all
x\in E
,

\displaystyle P_{n}\varphi_{1}(x)-R_{n}\varphi_{1}(x)=\mathbb{E}_{x}(\varphi_{1}(X_{n})\mathbbm{1}_{n<T_{K}})\leq\theta_{1}^{n}\varphi_{1}(x)

by Lemma
9.5
.
∎

We fix
1\leq k\leq n
,
h
such that
|h|\leq\varphi_{1}
and
\mu
such that
\mu(\varphi_{1})/\mu(\varphi_{2})\leq D_{\theta}
, where
\theta=\frac{1+\theta_{1}/\theta_{2}}{2}
and
D_{\theta}
is from Lemma
9.6
.
The inequality (
2.1
) with
\|\cdot\|_{TV}
in place of
\|\cdot\|_{TV(\varphi_{1})}
and Lemma
9.12
entail

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\mu P_{n-k}R_{k}h}{\mu P_{n-k}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}-\nu_{QSD}(R_{k}h)\right|\leq C\alpha^{n-k}\frac{\mu(\varphi_{1})}{\mu(\varphi_{2})}\sup_{x\in E}|R_{k}h(x)|\leq CD_{\theta}\bar{R}\alpha^{n-k}.

The second inequality of Lemma
9.12
implies

\displaystyle|\nu_{QSD}[(P_{k}-R_{k})h]|\leq\theta_{1}^{k}\nu_{QSD}(\varphi_{1})

and, by Lemma
9.6
,

\displaystyle\frac{\mu P_{n-k}(P_{k}-R_{k})h}{\mu P_{n-k}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}

\displaystyle\leq\theta_{1}^{k}\frac{\mu P_{n-k}\varphi_{1}}{\mu P_{n-k}\varphi_{2}}\leq\theta_{1}^{k}\left(D_{\theta}\vee\frac{\mu(\varphi_{1})}{\mu(\varphi_{2})}\right)=\theta_{1}^{k}D_{\theta}.

Combining the last three inequalities and recalling that
\nu_{QSD}P_{k}h=\theta_{0}^{k}\nu_{QSD}(h)
, we obtain that, for some constant
C>0
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\mu P_{n}h}{\theta_{0}^{k}\mu P_{n-k}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}-\nu_{QSD}(h)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\alpha^{n-k}\theta_{0}^{-k}+(\theta_{1}/\theta_{0})^{k}\right).

Applying the last inequality to
h=\mathbbm{1}_{E}
, we obtain

\left|\frac{1}{\theta_{0}^{k}\mu P_{n-k}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}-\frac{1}{\mu P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}\right|\leq\frac{C(\alpha^{n-k}\theta_{0}^{-k}+(\theta_{1}/\theta_{0})^{k})}{\mu P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}

so that, using Lemma
9.6
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\mu P_{n}h}{\theta_{0}^{k}\mu P_{n-k}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}-\frac{\mu P_{n}h}{\mu P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C(\alpha^{n-k}\theta_{0}^{-k}+(\theta_{1}/\theta_{0})^{k})\,\frac{\mu P_{n}\varphi_{1}}{\mu P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}\leq CD_{\theta}(\alpha^{n-k}\theta_{0}^{-k}+(\theta_{1}/\theta_{0})^{k}).

Hence, for some
\bar{\alpha}<1
, for all
n\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\mu P_{n}h}{\mu P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}-\nu_{QSD}(h)\right|\leq C(\alpha^{n-k}\theta_{0}^{-k}+(\theta_{1}/\theta_{0})^{k})\leq C\bar{\alpha}^{n}.

Finally, if
\mu(\varphi_{1})/\mu(\varphi_{2})>D_{\theta}
, then let
T=\left\lceil\frac{\ln(D_{\theta}\mu(\varphi_{1})/\mu(\varphi_{2}))}{-\ln\theta}\right\rceil
, so that
\mu P_{T}\varphi_{1}/\mu P_{T}\varphi_{2}\leq D_{\theta}
according to Lemma
9.6
. We deduce from the previous inequality applied to
\mu P_{T}/\mu P_{T}\mathbbm{1}_{E}
that, for all
n\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\mu P_{T+n}h}{\mu P_{T+n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}-\nu_{QSD}(h)\right|\leq C\bar{\alpha}^{n}\leq C\bar{\alpha}^{n}\theta^{T-1}\frac{\mu(\varphi_{1})}{\mu(\varphi_{2})}

while, using again Lemma
9.6
, we obtain, for all
n\in\{0,T-1\}
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\mu P_{n}h}{\mu P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}-\nu_{QSD}(h)\right|\leq D_{\theta}\vee\left(\theta^{n}\frac{\mu(\varphi_{1})}{\mu(\varphi_{2})}\right)+\nu_{QSD}(\varphi_{1})\leq C\theta^{n}\frac{\mu(\varphi_{1})}{\mu(\varphi_{2})}.

The last two inequalities conclude the proof of (
2.1
) with
\alpha=\bar{\alpha}\vee\theta
and hence of Theorem
2.1
.

9.8
The case where
\mathbb{P}_{x}(n<\tau_{\partial})=0
for some
x\in E
and
n\geq 1

In this section, we assume that
X
satisfies assumption (E), but we do not assume anymore that
\mathbb{P}_{x}(n<\tau_{\partial})>0
for
all
x\in E
and all
n\geq 1
. We introduce
\bar{E}=\{x\in E,\ \mathbb{P}_{x}(n<\tau_{\partial})>0\ \forall n\geq 0\}
and
\bar{\bar{E}}=E\setminus\bar{E}
. One immediately deduces from (E2) that, for all
x\in\bar{\bar{E}}
and all
n\geq 0
,
\varphi_{2}(x)=0
and
\mathbb{P}_{x}(X_{n}\in K)=0
, and hence that
\delta_{x}P_{n}\varphi_{1}\leq\theta_{1}^{n}\varphi_{1}(x)
by Lemma
9.5
. In addition, one easily checks that the semi-group
P
restricted to
\bar{E}\cup\{\partial\}
still satisfies assumption (E), and in particular (
2.1
) applies.

Let
\mu
be a probability measure on
E
such that
\mu(\varphi_{2})>0
and
\mu(\varphi_{1})<+\infty
. Then, for all
n\geq 0
and all
|h|\leq\varphi_{1}
,

\displaystyle\left|\mu P_{n}h-\nu_{QSD}(h)\mu P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\mu P_{n}h-\mu_{\rvert\bar{E}}P_{n}h\right|+\left|\mu_{\rvert\bar{E}}P_{n}h-\nu_{QSD}(h)\mu_{\rvert\bar{E}}P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad+\nu_{QSD}(\varphi_{1})\left|\mu_{\rvert\bar{E}}P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}-\mu P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}\right|.

Each term can be bounded as follows:

\displaystyle\left|\mu P_{n}h-\mu_{\rvert\bar{E}}P_{n}h\right|\leq\mu_{\rvert\bar{\bar{E}}}P_{n}\varphi_{1}\leq\theta_{1}^{n}\mu\varphi_{1},

\displaystyle\left|\mu_{\rvert\bar{E}}P_{n}h-\nu_{QSD}(h)\mu_{\rvert\bar{E}}P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}\right|\leq C\alpha^{n}\frac{\mu_{\rvert\bar{E}}(\varphi_{1})}{\mu_{\rvert\bar{E}}(\varphi_{2})}\mu_{\rvert\bar{E}}P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}\leq C\alpha^{n}\frac{\mu(\varphi_{1})}{\mu(\varphi_{2})}\mu P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E},

\displaystyle\nu_{QSD}(\varphi_{1})\left|\mu_{\rvert\bar{E}}P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}-\mu P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}\right|\leq\nu_{QSD}(\varphi_{1})\mu_{\rvert\bar{\bar{E}}}P_{n}\varphi_{1}\leq\nu_{QSD}(\varphi_{1})\theta_{1}^{n}\mu\varphi_{1}.

Since
\mu P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}\geq\theta_{2}^{n}\mu(\varphi_{2})
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\mu P_{n}h}{\mu P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}-\nu_{QSD}(h)\right|\leq\left((\theta_{1}/\theta_{2})^{n}+\nu_{QSD}(\varphi_{1})(\theta_{1}/\theta_{2})^{n}+C\alpha^{n}\right)\frac{\mu(\varphi_{1})}{\mu(\varphi_{2})}.

This concludes the proof of (
2.1
) in the general case.

10
Proof of the other results of Section
2

The previous section ensures the existence of a quasi-stationary distribution
\nu_{QSD}
such that
\nu_{QSD}(\varphi_{1})<+\infty
and
\nu_{QSD}(K)>0
. Denoting by
\theta_{0}
its associated decay parameter, we observe that
\theta_{2}\leq\theta_{0}
, since Lemma
9.5
entails that, for all
n\geq 1
,

\theta_{0}^{n}=\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{QSD}}(n<\tau_{\partial})\geq\nu_{QSD}(K)\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(n<\tau_{\partial})\geq\nu_{QSD}(K)\theta_{2}^{n}\inf_{y\in K}\varphi_{2}(y).

We begin to prove Theorem
2.2
in Section
10.1
, except for the exponential convergence in

L^{\infty}(\varphi_{1})
. We then prove Theorem
2.3
in Section
10.2
. In Section
10.3
,
we conclude the proof of Theorem
2.2
and prove Corollary
2.4
. We prove Corollary
2.6
in Subsection
10.5
.

10.1
Proof of the existence of the eigenfunction
\eta

In this section, we show that the limit (
2.2
) is well defined pointwise,
\nu_{QSD}(\eta)=1
,

P_{1}\eta=\theta_{0}\eta
,
\eta
is lower bounded away from
0
on
K
and
\eta\in L^{\infty}(\varphi_{1}^{\log(1/\theta_{0})/\log(1/\theta_{1})})
.

For all
n\geq 0
and
x\in E\cup\{\partial\}
, let us denote

\eta_{n}(x)=\theta_{0}^{-n}\mathbb{P}_{x}(n<\tau_{\partial})=\frac{\mathbb{P}_{x}(n<\tau_{\partial})}{\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{QSD}}(n<\tau_{\partial})}.

By Lemma
9.9
, for all
x\in E
,

\displaystyle\eta_{n}(x)

\displaystyle\leq C\theta_{0}^{-n}\inf_{y\in K}\mathbb{P}_{y}(n<\tau_{\partial})\varphi_{1}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\nu_{QSD}(K)}\theta_{0}^{-n}\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{QSD}}(n<\tau_{\partial})\varphi_{1}(x)=\frac{C\varphi_{1}(x)}{\nu_{QSD}(K)}.

(10.1)

This implies that the sequence
(\eta_{n})_{n\geq 0}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}(\varphi_{1})
.

For all probability measure
\mu
on
E
and for all
n,m\geq 0
, by Markov’s property,

\mu(\eta_{n+m})=\mu(\eta_{n})\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left[\theta_{0}^{-m}\mathbb{P}_{X_{n}}(m<\tau_{\partial})\mid n<\tau_{\partial}\right].

Hence, by Theorem
2.1
, for all
\mu
such that
\mu(\varphi_{2})>0
and
\mu(\varphi_{1})<+\infty
,

\displaystyle|\mu(\eta_{n+m})-\mu(\eta_{n})|

\displaystyle=\mu(\eta_{n})\left|\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(\eta_{m}(X_{n})\mid n<\tau_{\partial})-1\right|

\displaystyle=\mu(\eta_{n})\left|\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(\eta_{m}(X_{n})\mid n<\tau_{\partial})-\nu_{QSD}(\eta_{m})\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\mu(\varphi_{1})\alpha^{n}\frac{\mu(\varphi_{1})}{\mu(\varphi_{2})}.

For any
x\in E
, applying this result to
\mu=(\delta_{x}+\nu_{QSD})/2
, we deduce that

\displaystyle|\eta_{n+m}(x)-\eta_{n}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq C\varphi_{1}(x)^{2}\alpha^{n}.

This shows that
(\eta_{n}(x))_{n\geq 0}
is a Cauchy sequence and hence that, for all
x\in E
,

\displaystyle\eta(x)=\lim_{n\to+\infty}\theta_{0}^{-n}\mathbb{P}_{x}(n<\tau_{\partial})

and, by (
10.1
), that
\eta\in L^{\infty}(\varphi_{1})
.

Then, since
\eta_{n}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}(\varphi_{1})
, we deduce by dominated convergence that
\nu_{QSD}(\eta)=1
and that, for
all
x\in E
,

\displaystyle\delta_{x}P_{1}\eta=\lim_{n\to+\infty}\delta_{x}P_{1}\eta_{n}=\lim_{n\to+\infty}\theta_{0}\eta_{n+1}(x)=\theta_{0}\eta(x).

(10.2)

The fact that
\eta
is lower bounded away from
0
on
K
is an immediate consequence of Lemma
9.9

(integrating (
9.6
) with respect to
\nu_{QSD}(\mathrm{d}x)
) and the fact that
\nu_{QSD}(\varphi_{1})<+\infty
.

It only remains to prove that
\eta\in L^{\infty}\left(\varphi_{1}^{\log\theta_{0}/\log\theta_{1}}\right)
.
To prove this, we use the operator
R_{n}
introduced in (
9.19
). By Lemma
9.12
and using the fact that
\eta\in L^{\infty}(\varphi_{1})
, for all
x\in E
,

\displaystyle\eta(x)

\displaystyle=\theta_{0}^{-n}P_{n}\eta(x)\leq C\theta_{0}^{-n}\left[R_{n}\varphi_{1}(x)+(P_{n}-R_{n})\varphi_{1}(x))\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\bar{R}\theta_{0}^{-n}+C\left(\frac{\theta_{1}}{\theta_{0}}\right)^{n}\varphi_{1}(x).

Applying this inequality for
n=\lfloor-\log\varphi_{1}(x)/\log\theta_{1}\rfloor
, we deduce

\displaystyle\eta(x)\leq C\exp\left(\frac{\log\varphi_{1}(x)}{\log\theta_{1}}\,\log\theta_{0}\right)\leq C\varphi_{1}(x)^{\log\theta_{0}/\log\theta_{1}},

which concludes the proof.

10.2
Proof of Theorem
2.3

We start with Point (i). We introduce
\Gamma_{n}=\mathbbm{1}_{n<\tau_{\partial}}
and define for all
x\in E^{\prime}
and
n\geq 0
the probability measure

\displaystyle Q^{\Gamma,x}_{n}=\frac{\Gamma_{n}}{\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\Gamma_{n}\right)}\mathbb{P}_{x},

so that the
Q
-process exists if and only if
Q_{n}^{\Gamma,x}
admits a proper limit when
n\rightarrow\infty
. For all
0\leq k\leq n
, we have by the Markov property

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\Gamma_{n}\mid{\cal F}_{k}\right)}{\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\Gamma_{n}\right)}=\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{k<\tau_{\partial}}\mathbb{P}_{X_{k}}\left(n-k<\tau_{\partial}\right)}{\mathbb{P}_{x}\left(n<\tau_{\partial}\right)}.

By the pointwise convergence in (
2.2
) (proved in Subsection
10.1
), this converges almost surely
as
n\rightarrow+\infty
to

\displaystyle M_{k}:=\mathbbm{1}_{k<\tau_{\partial}}\theta_{0}^{-k}\frac{\eta(X_{k})}{\eta(x)}=\theta_{0}^{-k}\frac{\eta(X_{k})}{\eta(x)},

and
\mathbb{E}_{x}(M_{k})=\theta_{0}^{-k}\frac{P_{k}\eta(x)}{\eta(x)}=1
. These two properties allow to apply the penalization’s theorem of
Roynette, Vallois and Yor
[
94
, Theorem 2.1]
, which implies that
M
is a martingale under
\mathbb{P}_{x}
and that

Q_{n}^{\Gamma,x}(A)
converges to
\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(M_{k}\mathbbm{1}_{A}\right)
for all
A\in{\cal F}_{k}
when
n\rightarrow\infty
. This means that

\mathbb{Q}_{x}
is well defined and

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{Q}_{x}}{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{x}}\,\smash{\vrule height=5.55557pt,depth=2.17694pt}}_{\,{\cal F}_{k}}}{{\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{Q}_{x}}{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{x}}\,\smash{\vrule height=3.88889pt,depth=2.17694pt}}_{\,{\cal F}_{k}}}{{\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{Q}_{x}}{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{x}}\,\smash{\vrule height=2.77777pt,depth=2.34222pt}}_{\,{\cal F}_{k}}}{{\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{Q}_{x}}{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{x}}\,\smash{\vrule height=2.77777pt,depth=2.92778pt}}_{\,{\cal F}_{k}}}=M_{k}.

Note that the fact that
\eta(x)=0
for all
x\in E\setminus E^{\prime}
implies that
(X_{n},n\geq 0)
is
E^{\prime}
-valued

\mathbb{Q}_{x}
-almost surely for all
x\in E^{\prime}
.
The fact that
X
is Markov under
(\mathbb{Q}_{x})_{x\in E^{\prime}}
and Point (ii) can be easily deduced from the last formula (see
e.g.
[
20
, Section 6.1]
).

It remains to prove Point (iii).
We define the function
\psi=\varphi_{1}/\eta\times\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}(\varphi_{1})}
on
E^{\prime}
. Note that, since
\eta\in L^{\infty}(\varphi_{1})
,
\psi
is uniformly lower
bounded. Moreover, for all
x\in E^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}_{1}\psi(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{\theta_{0}^{-1}\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}(\varphi_{1})}}{\eta(x)}P_{1}\varphi_{1}(x)\leq\frac{\theta_{1}}{\theta_{0}}\psi(x)+\frac{c_{2}\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}(\varphi_{1})}}{\theta_{0}\eta(x)}\mathbbm{1}_{K}(x)\leq\widetilde{\theta}\psi(x)+\widetilde{c},

where
\widetilde{\theta}=\theta_{1}/\theta_{0}
and

\widetilde{c}=\frac{c_{2}\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}(\varphi_{1})}}{\theta_{0}\inf_{K}\eta}.

Hence, for all
x\in E
and all
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}_{n}\psi(x)

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{\theta}\widetilde{P}_{n-1}\psi(x)+\widetilde{c}\leq...\leq{\widetilde{\theta}}^{n}\psi(x)+\frac{\widetilde{c}}{1-\widetilde{\theta}}.

(10.3)

Using Lemma
9.5
, we have that, for all
x\in E^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{x}(T_{K}>n)=\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\theta_{0}^{-n}\frac{\varphi_{1}(X_{n})}{\eta(X_{n})}\mathbbm{1}_{T_{K}>n}\mathbbm{1}_{X_{n}\in E^{\prime}}\right)\leq\widetilde{\theta}^{n}\psi(x)

(10.4)

Now, choosing
m_{K}
large enough so that
\sup_{x\in K}\widetilde{\theta}^{m_{K}}[\sup_{K}\psi+\tilde{c}/(1-\tilde{\theta})]\leq 1/2
, we deduce that, for all
x\in K
and all
n_{0}\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{x}(\exists n\in\{n_{0},\ldots,n_{0}+m_{K}\},\ X_{n}\in K)\geq 1-\widetilde{\theta}^{m_{K}}\widetilde{P}_{n_{0}}\psi(x)\geq 1/2.

(10.5)

Now, let
n_{K}\geq 1
be such that
\inf_{x\in K}\mathbb{P}_{x}(X_{n}\in K)>0
for all
n\geq n_{K}
(such a
n_{K}
exists by (E1) and (E4), see the
proof of Lemma
9.7
) and let

a:=\inf_{n\in\{n_{K},\ldots,n_{K}+m_{K}\}}\inf_{x\in K}\mathbb{P}_{x}(X_{n}\in K)>0.

so that, for all
x\in K
, all
n\in\{n_{K},\ldots,n_{K}+m_{K}\}
and all
A\subset E
measurable,

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{x}(X_{n+n_{1}}\in A)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{Q}_{x}(X_{n}\in K,X_{n+n_{1}}\in A)=\frac{\theta_{0}^{-n-n_{1}}}{\eta(x)}\mathbb{E}_{x}(\mathbbm{1}_{X_{n}\in K}\mathbb{E}_{X_{n}}(\eta(X_{n_{1}})\mathbbm{1}_{X_{n_{1}}\in A}))

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\theta_{0}^{-n-n_{1}}}{\eta(x)}ac_{1}\nu(\eta\mathbbm{1}_{A})=\frac{\theta_{0}^{-n-n_{1}}\nu(\eta)}{\eta(x)}ac_{1}\nu_{\eta}(A)\geq\frac{ac_{1}}{c_{3}}\nu_{\eta}(A),

where we used (E1) and (E3) and defined
\nu_{\eta}(\mathrm{d}x):=\frac{\eta(x)\nu(\mathrm{d}x)}{\nu(\eta)}
.
We deduce from the last inequality and (
10.5
) that, for all
n_{0}\geq 0
,

\mathbb{Q}_{x}(X_{n_{0}+n_{1}+n_{K}+m_{K}}\in\cdot)\geq\sum_{n=n_{0}}^{n_{0}+m_{K}}\mathbb{Q}_{x}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{X_{n}\in K}\mathbb{Q}_{X_{n}}(X_{n_{0}+n_{1}+n_{K}+m_{K}-n}\in\cdot)\right]\geq\frac{ac_{1}}{2c_{3}}\nu_{\eta}.

Hence, for all
n\geq n_{K}+m_{K}+n_{1}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{x}(X_{n}\in\cdot)\geq\frac{ac_{1}}{2c_{3}}\nu_{\eta},\quad\forall x\in K.

For all
x\in E^{\prime}
, setting
k_{x}=\lceil\frac{\log(2\psi(x))}{-\log\widetilde{\theta}}\rceil
, it follows from (
10.4
) that

\mathbb{Q}_{x}(T_{K}\leq k_{x})\geq\frac{1}{2},

and hence

\mathbb{Q}_{x}(X_{k_{x}+n_{K}+m_{K}+n_{1}}\in\cdot)\geq\frac{ac_{1}}{4c_{3}}\nu_{\eta}.

In particular, for all
R>0
, setting
k_{R}=\lceil\frac{\log(2R)}{-\log\widetilde{\theta}}\rceil+n_{K}+m_{k}+n_{1}
, we have, for all
x,y\in E^{\prime}
such that
\psi(x)+\psi(y)<R
,

\left\|\delta_{x}P_{k_{R}}-\delta_{y}P_{k_{R}}\right\|_{TV}\leq 1-\frac{ac_{1}}{4c_{3}}.

(10.6)

By
[
57
, Thm 3.9]
, together with (
10.3
), the last assertion implies that there exist
constants
C>0
and
\widetilde{\alpha}_{1}\in(0,1)
such that, for all real function
h
on
E^{\prime}
such that
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|h\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}<\infty
,

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\widetilde{P}_{n}h\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}\leq C\widetilde{\alpha}_{1}^{n}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|h\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|},

(10.7)

where

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|h\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}=\sup_{x,y\in E^{\prime}}\frac{|h(x)-h(y)|}{2+\psi(x)+\psi(y)}.

This implies (
2.4
).
In particular, for all
x\in E^{\prime}
,

\|\delta_{x}\widetilde{P}_{n}-\beta\|_{TV}\xrightarrow[n\rightarrow+\infty]{}0.

Hence, (
2.5
) is a consequence of Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem. This ends the proof of
Theorem
2.3
.

Remark 22
.

As noted in
[
57
, Remark 3.10]
, it is possible to obtain explicit constants
\widetilde{C}
and

\widetilde{\alpha}_{1}
in (
10.7
) from the parameters in (
10.3
) and (
10.6
)
(note that a slight modification of the proof of Lemma
9.9
entails that one can actually take

\widetilde{c}=\frac{1/\theta_{1}}{1-\theta_{1}/\theta_{0}}\leq\frac{1/\theta_{1}}{1-\theta_{1}/\theta_{2}}
). More precisely, setting

\alpha=\frac{ac_{1}}{4c_{3}}
and
K=\widetilde{c}/(1-\widetilde{\theta})
and
\gamma=\widetilde{\theta}
, then taking any

\alpha_{0}\in(0,\alpha)
and
R>\frac{2K}{1-\gamma}
, and setting
b=\frac{2\alpha_{0}}{\gamma R+2K}
,

\alpha_{R}=(1-\alpha+\alpha_{0})\vee\frac{2+b\gamma R+b\gamma K}{2+bR}\in(0,1),

and
C_{R}=\frac{2/b+1+K+K/(1-\gamma)}{\alpha_{R}}
,
we obtain that, for all
f\in L^{\infty}(\varphi_{1}/\eta)
,

\left|\widetilde{P}_{n}f-\beta(f)\right|\leq C_{R}\alpha_{R}^{n/k_{R}}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\varphi_{1}/\eta)}.

10.3
Proof of Corollary
2.4
and end of the proof of Theorem
2.2

Let
|g|\leq\varphi_{1}
and set
h=g/\eta
. Then (
2.4
) entails that, for all
x\in E^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\left|\theta_{0}^{-n}\mathbb{E}_{x}(g(X_{n})\mathbbm{1}_{X_{n}\in E^{\prime}})-\eta(x)\nu_{QSD}(g\mathbbm{1}_{E^{\prime}})\right|\leq C\,\bar{\alpha}^{n}\varphi_{1}(x).

In what follows, we set
\nu^{\prime}=\nu_{QSD}(\cdot\cap{E^{\prime}})
and, for all
k\geq 1
,

g_{k}(x)=\mathbbm{1}_{x\in E^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{X_{1}\notin E^{\prime}}g(X_{k})\mathbbm{1}_{k<\tau_{\partial}}\right).

Note that, defining
E^{\prime\prime}:=E\setminus E^{\prime}
,
E^{\prime\prime}\cup\{\partial\}
is an absorbing set. Since
K\subset E^{\prime}
, we thus have

g_{k}(x)\leq\mathbbm{1}_{x\in E^{\prime}}\,\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{k<T_{K}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}\varphi_{1}(X_{k})\right)\leq\theta_{1}^{k}\varphi_{1}(x).

We also define the measure
\nu^{\prime\prime}
on
E^{\prime\prime}
by

\nu^{\prime\prime}=\sum_{\ell\geq 1}\theta_{0}^{-\ell}\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{\prime}}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{X_{1}\notin E^{\prime}}\mathbbm{1}_{X_{\ell}\in\cdot}\right)=\sum_{\ell\geq 1}\theta_{0}^{-\ell}\nu^{\prime}(g_{\ell}).

Hence we have for all
n\geq 1
and all
x\in E^{\prime}

\displaystyle\left|\theta_{0}^{-n}\mathbb{E}_{x}(g(X_{n})\mathbbm{1}_{X_{n}\in E^{\prime\prime}})-\eta(x)\nu^{\prime\prime}(g)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left|\theta_{0}^{-n}\mathbb{E}_{x}(g_{\ell}(X_{n-\ell}))-\theta_{0}^{-\ell}\eta(x)\nu^{\prime}(g_{\ell})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\theta_{0}^{-\ell}\left|\theta_{0}^{-(n-\ell)}\mathbb{E}_{x}(g_{\ell}(X_{n-\ell}))-\eta(x)\nu^{\prime}(g_{\ell})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\theta_{0}^{-\ell}\varphi_{1}(x)C\bar{\alpha}^{n-\ell}\|g_{\ell}\|_{L^{\infty}(\varphi_{1})}

\displaystyle\leq C\varphi_{1}(x)\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left(\frac{\theta_{1}}{\theta_{0}}\right)^{\ell}\bar{\alpha}^{n-\ell}.

We thus proved that, setting
\nu_{0}=\nu^{\prime}+\nu^{\prime\prime}
, and up to a change in the constants
C
and
\bar{\alpha}
, for all
x\in E^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\left|\theta_{0}^{-n}\mathbb{E}_{x}(g(X_{n})\mathbbm{1}_{n<\tau_{\partial}})-\eta(x)\nu_{0}(g)\right|\leq C\,\bar{\alpha}^{n}\varphi_{1}(x).

Now, by Lemma
9.5
, for all
x\in^{\prime\prime}
,
|\mathbb{E}_{x}g(X_{n})\mathbbm{1}_{n<\tau_{\partial}}|\leq\theta_{1}^{n}\varphi_{1}(x)
.
This, we have proved that, up to a change in
\bar{\alpha}
, for all
x\in E
,

\displaystyle\left|\theta_{0}^{-n}\mathbb{E}_{x}(g(X_{n}))-\eta(x)\nu_{0}(g)\right|\leq C\,\bar{\alpha}^{n}\varphi_{1}(x).

(10.8)

Integrating with respect to
\nu_{QSD}
shows that
\nu_{0}=\nu_{QSD}
.
Thus, we have proved (
2.6
).

To conclude, we can now end the proof of Theorem
2.2
. Indeed, taking
g\equiv 1
immediately entails that the
convergence (
2.2
) is geometric in
L^{\infty}(\varphi_{1})
.

10.4
Proof of Corollary
2.5

If
\eta(x)>0
, it follows from Corollary
2.4
and the fact that
\nu_{QSD}(\varphi_{2})>0
that there exists
k\geq 0

such that
\delta_{x}P_{k}\varphi_{2}>0
. Hence
E^{\prime}\subset\{x\in E:\exists k\geq 0,\ P_{k}\varphi_{2}(x)>0\}
. Conversely, if

P_{k}\varphi_{2}(x)>0
, we apply Theorem
2.1
to
\mu=\frac{\delta_{x}P_{k}}{\delta_{x}P_{k}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}
. Since

\nu_{QSD}(\eta)>0
, there exists
n\geq 0
such that

0<\frac{\mu P_{n}\eta}{\mu P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}=\frac{\delta_{x}P_{n+k}\eta}{\delta_{x}P_{n+k}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}=\frac{\theta_{0}^{n+k}}{\delta_{x}P_{n+k}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}\eta(x)
. Hence we have proved that
E^{\prime}=\{x\in E:\exists k\geq 0,\ P_{k}\varphi_{2}(x)>0\}
.

The fact that any
\mu
such that
\mu(E^{\prime})>0
and
\mu(\varphi_{1}^{1/p})<+\infty
for some
p<\log\theta_{1}/\log\theta_{2}

belongs to the domain of attraction of
\nu_{QSD}
follows from Remark
1
and Corollary
2.4
.

In the case where
\varphi_{1}
is bounded, the domain of atttraction contains all measures
\mu
such that
\mu(E^{\prime})>0
. If

\mu(\eta)=0
, then
\mu P_{k}\eta=0
for all
k\geq 0
, which means that
\mu P_{k}
gives no mass to
E^{\prime}
. Hence the convergence of
conditional distributions to
\nu_{QSD}
cannot hold true. The uniqueness of the quasi-stationary distribution follows immediately.

10.5
Proof of Corollary
2.6

Applying (
2.5
) with
\mu(\eta\cdot)/\mu(\eta)
instead of
\mu
and recalling that

\mu(E\setminus E^{\prime})=0
, we obtain

\sup_{f:E^{\prime}\to\mathbb{R},\,\|f\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\left|\theta_{0}^{-n}\frac{\mu P_{n}(\eta f)}{\mu(\eta)}-\beta(f)\right|\xrightarrow[n\rightarrow+\infty]{}0.

This entails the convergence result (
2.7
).

Assume from now on that
\eta
is positive on
E
. Let
\nu
be a quasi-stationary distribution on
E
such that
\nu(\eta)<+\infty
and
denote by
\bar{\theta}_{0}\in(0,1]
the associated decay parameter, such that
\mathbb{P}_{\nu}(X_{n}\in\cdot)=\bar{\theta}_{0}^{n}\nu
for all
n\geq 0
. Then, according to (
2.7
), we have, for all
g\in L^{\infty}(\eta)
,

\displaystyle\left|\theta_{0}^{-n}\bar{\theta}_{0}^{n}\nu(g)-\nu(\eta)\nu_{QSD}(g)\right|\xrightarrow[n\to+\infty]{}0.

This entails that
\bar{\theta}_{0}=\theta_{0}
and that
\nu
is proportional to
\nu_{QSD}
. Since they both are probability measures, we deduce that
\nu=\nu_{QSD}
, which concludes the proof of the second claim of Corollary
2.6
.

Finally, assuming that
\eta
is lower bounded away from
0
on
E
, we deduce from (
2.7
) with
g\equiv 1
that, for all probability measure
\mu
on
E
such that
\mu(\eta)<+\infty
,

\displaystyle\theta_{0}^{-n}\mu P^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}\xrightarrow[n\to+\infty]{}\mu(\eta)>0.

This and (
2.7
) imply that

\sup_{g:E\to\mathbb{R},\,\|g\|_{L^{\infty}(\eta)}\leq 1}\left|\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(g(X_{n})\mid n<\tau_{\partial})-\nu_{QSD}(g)\right|\xrightarrow[n\to+\infty]{}0,

hence (
2.8
) holds true and the proof of Corollary
2.6
is completed.

11
Proof of the results of Section
3

In this section are proved Lemma
3.1
in Subsection
11.1
, Lemma
3.2
in
Subsection
11.2
, Proposition
3.3
in Subsection
11.3
and
Lemma
3.4
in Subsection
11.4
. Then we prove Theorem
3.5
in
Subsection
11.5
, Lemma
3.6
in Subsection
11.6
and
Proposition
3.7
in Subsection
11.7
.

11.1
Proof of Lemma
3.1

The function
\varphi_{2}
defined in the statement satisfies, for all
x\in E
,
\varphi_{2}(x)\in[0,1]
and, for all
x\in K
,
\varphi_{2}(x)\geq\frac{\theta^{-1}_{2}-1}{\theta_{2}^{-\ell}-1}>0
. Moreover, we have, for all
x\in E
,

\displaystyle P_{1}\varphi_{2}(x)

\displaystyle=\theta_{2}\varphi_{2}(x)-\frac{\theta^{-1}_{2}-1}{\theta_{2}^{-\ell}-1}\,\left(\theta_{2}\mathbbm{1}_{K}(x)-\theta_{2}^{-\ell+1}P_{\ell}\mathbbm{1}_{K}(x)\right)\geq\theta_{2}\varphi_{2}(x)

since
\ell
is chosen such that
\theta_{2}^{-\ell}P_{\ell}\mathbbm{1}_{K}(x)\geq\mathbbm{1}_{K}(x)
for all
x\in E
.

Our assumption also implies that there exists
n_{0}
such that, for all
n\geq n_{0}
,
\theta_{2}^{-n}\inf_{x\in K}\mathbb{P}_{x}(X_{n}\in K)\geq 1
. Choosing
n_{4}(x)=n_{0}
for all
x\in K
entails (E4), which concludes the proof of Lemma
3.1
.

11.2
Proof of Lemma
3.2

Assume that

\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\theta_{1}^{-T_{K}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}\right)<+\infty\,\ \forall x\in E\text{ and }\sup_{y\in K}\,\mathbb{E}_{y}\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}}\left(\theta_{1}^{-T_{K}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{1<\tau_{\partial}}\right)<+\infty

and set
\varphi_{1}(x)=\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\theta_{1}^{-T_{K}\wedge\lceil\tau_{\partial}\rceil}\right)
for all
x\in E
. Then, for all
x\in E\setminus K
, using Markov’s property at time
1
,

\displaystyle P_{1}\varphi_{1}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}}\left(\theta_{1}^{-T_{K}\wedge\lceil\tau_{\partial}\rceil}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{1<\tau_{\partial}}\right)\leq\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\theta_{1}^{-(T_{K}\wedge\lceil\tau_{\partial}\rceil-1)}\right)=\theta_{1}\varphi_{1}(x).

Moreover, for all
x\in K
,
P_{1}\varphi_{1}(x)\leq\theta_{1}^{-1}\sup_{y\in K}\mathbb{E}_{y}\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}}\left(\theta_{1}^{-T_{K}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{1<\tau_{\partial}}\right)
, and hence the first part of the lemma is proved.

Assume now that there exist two constants
C>0
,
\theta_{1}>0
and a function
\varphi_{1}:E\to[1,+\infty)
such that
\sup_{K}\varphi_{1}<+\infty
and
P_{1}\varphi_{1}\leq\theta_{1}\varphi_{1}+C\mathbbm{1}_{K}
. Then, for all
n\geq 1
and all
x\in E\setminus K
,

\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\varphi_{1}(X_{n})\mathbbm{1}_{n<T_{K}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}\right)\leq\theta_{1}^{n}\varphi_{1}(x).

Thus we deduce that, for all
x\in E
,

\mathbb{P}_{x}\left(n<T_{K}\wedge\tau_{\partial}\right)\leq\theta_{1}^{n}\varphi_{1}(x).

In particular, for all
\theta>\theta_{1}
and all
x\in E
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\theta^{-T_{K}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}\right)\leq\frac{1}{\theta-\theta_{1}}\,\varphi_{1}(x)<+\infty.

We also deduce that

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in K}\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}}\left(\theta^{-T_{K}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}\right)\right)\leq\frac{1}{\theta-\theta_{1}}\sup_{x\in K}P_{1}\varphi_{1}(x)<+\infty.

This concludes the proof of Lemma
3.2
.

11.3
Proof of Proposition
3.3

Condition (E4) implies that there exists
x_{0}\in E
such that

\mathbb{P}_{x_{0}}(X_{n_{0}}\in K)>0
. We then deduce from our assumption (
3.2
) that Condition (E1) is satisfied with the
probability measure
\nu
on
K
defined by

\nu(\cdot)=\frac{\mathbb{P}_{x_{0}}(X_{n_{0}}\in\cdot\cap K)}{\mathbb{P}_{x_{0}}(X_{n_{0}}\in K)}

and the constants
c_{1}=\mathbb{P}_{x_{0}}(X_{n_{0}}\in K)/C>0
and
n_{1}=m_{0}
.

Let us now check Condition (E3) and the last part of Proposition
3.3
. We define
T_{K}^{(n_{0})}=\inf\{n\geq n_{0}\text{
s.t. }X_{n}\in K\}
. Lemma
9.5
(which only makes use of Condition (E2)) implies that, for all
x\in E
,

\mathbb{P}_{x}(n<T_{K}\wedge\tau_{\partial})\leq\theta_{1}^{n}\varphi_{1}(x)
. Hence, for all
x\in E
and all
n\geq n_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}(n<\tau_{\partial}\wedge T_{K}^{(n_{0})})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{n_{0}<\tau_{\partial}}\mathbb{P}_{X_{n_{0}}}(n-n_{0}<\tau_{\partial}\wedge T_{K})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\theta_{1}^{n-n_{0}}\,\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{n_{0}<\tau_{\partial}}\varphi_{1}(X_{n_{0}})\right)

\displaystyle\leq(\theta_{1}+c_{2})^{n_{0}}\theta_{1}^{n-n_{0}}\,\varphi_{1}(x).

Since
\varphi_{1}\geq 1
, we also have
\mathbb{P}_{x}(n<\tau_{\partial})\leq C\theta_{1}^{n}\varphi_{1}(x)
for all
n<n_{0}
. Hence we
proved that, for all
x\in E
and
n\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}_{x}(n<\tau_{\partial}\wedge T_{K}^{(n_{0})})\leq C\theta_{1}^{n}\varphi_{1}(x).

(11.1)

Therefore, for some constant
C>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}(n<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{x}(n<\tau_{\partial}\wedge T^{(n_{0})}_{K})+\mathbb{P}_{x}(T^{(n_{0})}_{K}\leq n<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\leq C\,\varphi_{1}(x)\theta_{1}^{n}+\sum_{k=n_{0}}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{T^{(n_{0})}_{K}=k}\mathbb{P}_{X_{k}}(n-k<\tau_{\partial})\right).

(11.2)

Now, for all
x\in E
, all
y\in K
and all
k\in\{n_{0},\ldots,n\}
, (
3.2
) and (
11.1
) entail

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{T^{(n_{0})}_{K}=k}\mathbb{P}_{X_{k}}(n-k<\tau_{\partial})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{k-n_{0}<T_{K}^{(n_{0})}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{k-n_{0}}}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{X_{n_{0}}\in K}\,\mathbb{P}_{X_{n_{0}}}(n-k<\tau_{\partial})\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{k-n_{0}<T^{(n_{0})}_{K}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}C\,\mathbb{P}_{y}(n+m_{0}-k<\tau_{\partial})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\theta_{1}^{k-n_{0}}\varphi_{1}(x)\,C\,\mathbb{P}_{y}(n-k<\tau_{\partial}),

where the constant
C
may change from line to line.
Using Lemma
9.8
, which only makes use of (E1), (E2) and (E4), there exists
n_{6}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}

such that, for all
y\in K
and for all
n,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
such that
n-k\geq n_{6}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{y}(n<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}_{y}(X_{n-k}\in K)\inf_{z\in K}\mathbb{P}_{z}(k<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}_{y}(n-k<\tau_{\partial})\,\inf_{T\geq n_{6}}\inf_{z\in K}\mathbb{P}_{z}(X_{T}\in K\mid T<\tau_{\partial})\,\inf_{z\in K}P_{k}\varphi_{2}(z)

\displaystyle\geq C^{\prime\prime}\theta_{2}^{k}\,\mathbb{P}_{y}(n-k<\tau_{\partial}),

where
C^{\prime\prime}>0
. Hence,

\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{T^{(n_{0})}_{K}=k}\mathbb{P}_{X_{k}}(n-k<\tau_{\partial})\right)\leq\varphi_{1}(x)\,\left(\frac{\theta_{1}}{\theta_{2}}\right)^{k}\,\frac{\theta_{1}^{-n_{0}}\,C}{C^{\prime\prime}}\,\mathbb{P}_{y}(n<\tau_{\partial}).

Now, we deduce from (
11.2
) and (
11.1
) that, for all
x\in E
and all
y\in K
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}(n<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\leq C\,\varphi_{1}(x)\,\left[\theta_{1}^{n}+\mathbb{P}_{y}(n<\tau_{\partial})\,\,\sum_{k=1}^{n-n_{6}}\left(\frac{\theta_{1}}{\theta_{2}}\right)^{k}\right]+\mathbb{P}_{x}(T_{K}^{(n_{0})}\wedge\tau_{\partial}\geq n-n_{6})

\displaystyle\leq C\,\varphi_{1}(x)\,\left[\theta_{1}^{n}+\mathbb{P}_{y}(n<\tau_{\partial})+\theta_{1}^{n-n_{6}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\,\varphi_{1}(x)\,\mathbb{P}_{y}(n<\tau_{\partial})

since
\mathbb{P}_{y}(n<\tau_{\partial})\geq\theta_{2}^{n}\,\inf_{K}\varphi_{2}
. This implies (E3) since

\sup_{K}\varphi_{1}<\infty
.

11.4
Proof of Lemma
3.4

Combining Theorem
2.2
and the fact that
\inf_{K}\eta>0
, we deduce that

\liminf_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\ \inf_{x\in K}\theta_{0}^{-n}\mathbb{P}_{x}(n<\tau_{\partial})>0.

Let
\theta^{\prime}_{2}<\theta_{0}
. Using Lemma
9.8
,

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\,(\theta^{\prime}_{2})^{-n}\,\inf_{x\in K}\mathbb{P}_{x}(X_{n}\in K)=+\infty.

Hence the result follows from Lemma
3.1
.

11.5
Proof of Theorem
3.5

We assume that Assumption (F) is satisfied. In Subsection
11.5.1
, we prove that Assumption (E) holds true for the
sub-Markovian semigroup
(P_{n})_{n\geq 0}
of the absorbed Markov process
(X_{nt_{2}},n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+})
. In Subsection
11.5.2
, we
prove the existence of a quasi-stationary distribution for
(X_{t})_{t\in I}
with the claimed properties and in
Subsection
11.5.3
, we prove the convergence of
e^{\lambda_{0}t}\mathbb{P}_{x}(t<\tau_{\partial})
to
\eta(x)
for
t\in I
,

t\rightarrow+\infty
.

11.5.1
Proof of (E)

We fix
\theta_{1}\in(\gamma_{1}^{t_{2}},\gamma_{2}^{t_{2}})
and set
\theta_{2}=\gamma_{2}^{t_{2}}
. Let us first remark that the last line of
Condition (F2) implies that
\gamma_{2}^{-t}\mathbb{P}_{\nu}(X_{t}\in L)\rightarrow+\infty
when
t\rightarrow+\infty
. Hence, using
Condition (F1), we deduce that

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}^{-t}\inf_{x\in L}\mathbb{P}_{x}(X_{t}\in L)\xrightarrow[t\rightarrow+\infty]{}+\infty.

(11.3)

We consider a number
n_{0}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
large enough so that
\gamma_{2}^{-t}\inf_{x\in L}\mathbb{P}_{x}(X_{t}\in L)\geq 1\vee\frac{c_{2}}{\theta_{1}-\gamma_{1}^{t_{2}}}
, for all
t\geq(n_{0}-1)t_{2}
and we set

\varphi_{1}=\psi_{1}\quad\text{ and }\quad\varphi_{2}=\frac{\gamma_{2}^{-t_{2}}-1}{\gamma_{2}^{-n_{0}t_{2}}-1}\sum_{k=0}^{n_{0}-1}\gamma_{2}^{-kt_{2}}P_{k}\mathbbm{1}_{L}.

Step 1. Proof of (E2), (E4) and (E1) for
(P_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
.

For all
x\in E\setminus L
, it follows from (F0) and the second line of (F2) that

\displaystyle P_{1}\psi_{1}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\psi_{1}(X_{t_{2}})\mathbbm{1}_{t_{2}<\tau_{L}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}\right)+\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\tau_{L}\leq t_{2}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}\mathchoice{{\mathbb{E}_{X_{\tau_{L}}}(\mathbbm{1}_{t_{2}-s<\tau_{\partial}}\psi_{1}(X_{t_{2}-s}))\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=3.56529pt}}_{\,s=\tau_{L}}}{{\mathbb{E}_{X_{\tau_{L}}}(\mathbbm{1}_{t_{2}-s<\tau_{\partial}}\psi_{1}(X_{t_{2}-s}))\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=3.56529pt}}_{\,s=\tau_{L}}}{{\mathbb{E}_{X_{\tau_{L}}}(\mathbbm{1}_{t_{2}-s<\tau_{\partial}}\psi_{1}(X_{t_{2}-s}))\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=2.93251pt}}_{\,s=\tau_{L}}}{{\mathbb{E}_{X_{\tau_{L}}}(\mathbbm{1}_{t_{2}-s<\tau_{\partial}}\psi_{1}(X_{t_{2}-s}))\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.51807pt}}_{\,s=\tau_{L}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{1}^{t_{2}}\psi_{1}(x)+\mathbb{P}_{x}(\tau_{L}\leq t_{2})c_{2}.

We define

K=\left\{y\in E,\ \mathbb{P}_{y}(\tau_{L}\leq t_{2})/\psi_{1}(y)\geq(\theta_{1}-\gamma_{1}^{t_{2}})/c_{2}\right\}.

The second line of (F2) at time
t=0
and the fact that
\theta_{1}-\gamma_{1}^{t_{2}}<1
imply that
L\subset K
. Moreover, we have, for
all
x\notin K
,

\displaystyle P_{1}\psi_{1}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\theta_{1}\psi_{1}(x).

(11.4)

Hence, for all
x\in E
,

P_{1}\psi_{1}(x)\leq\theta_{1}\psi_{1}(x)+c_{2}\mathbbm{1}_{K}(x).

(11.5)

Note that it immediately follows from the definition of
K
that
\sup_{x\in K}\psi_{1}(x)<\infty
. In particular, the first and third
lines of (E2) are proved.

Moreover, using the Markov property provided by (F0) and the definition of
n_{0}
, we deduce that, for all
t\geq n_{0}t_{2}
,

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in K}\gamma_{2}^{-t}\mathbb{P}_{x}(X_{t}\in L)

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{x\in K}\mathbb{P}_{x}(\tau_{L}\leq t_{2})\inf_{s\in[0,t_{2}]}\inf_{y\in L}\gamma_{2}^{-t}\mathbb{P}_{y}\left(X_{t-s}\in L\right)\geq 1,

(11.6)

where we used the fact that, for all
x\in K
,

\mathbb{P}_{x}(\tau_{L}\leq t_{2})\geq\frac{\theta_{1}-\gamma_{1}^{t_{2}}}{c_{2}}
. In particular,

\displaystyle P_{1}\varphi_{2}=\gamma_{2}^{t_{2}}\varphi_{2}+\frac{\gamma_{2}^{-t_{2}}-1}{\gamma_{2}^{-n_{0}t_{2}}-1}\left(\gamma_{2}^{-(n_{0}-1)t_{2}}P_{n_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{L}-\gamma_{2}^{t_{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{L}\right)\geq\gamma_{2}^{t_{2}}\varphi_{2}=\theta_{2}\varphi_{2}.

In addition, for all
x\in K
,

\varphi_{2}(x)\geq\frac{\gamma_{2}^{-t_{2}}-1}{\gamma_{2}^{-n_{0}t_{2}}-1}\gamma_{2}^{-(n_{0}-1)t_{2}}\mathbb{P}_{x}(X_{n_{0}t_{2}}\in L)\geq\frac{1-\gamma_{2}^{t_{2}}}{\gamma_{2}^{-n_{0}t_{2}}-1}.

Hence (E2) is proved. Moreover, (
11.6
) also entails that (E4) holds true.

Fix
n_{1}>n_{0}
such that

n_{1}t_{2}-t_{1}\geq n_{0}t_{2}
. Condition (F1) and then (
11.6
) imply that, for all
x\in K
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}(X_{n_{1}t_{2}}\in\cdot\cap K)\geq\mathbb{P}_{x}(X_{n_{1}t_{2}-t_{1}}\in L)c_{1}\nu(\cdot\cap L)\geq\gamma_{2}^{n_{1}t_{2}-t_{1}}c_{1}\nu(\cdot\cap L).

Extending
\nu
as a probability measure on
K
, we obtain (E1).

Step 3. Estimation of the survival probability.

Our goal here is to prove a version of Lemma
9.9
, where (
9.6
) is replaced by

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}(nt_{2}<\tau_{\partial})\leq C\frac{\varphi_{1}(x)}{1-\theta_{1}/\theta_{2}}\inf_{y\in L}\mathbb{P}_{y}(nt_{2}<\tau_{\partial}),\quad\forall x\in E,\forall n\in\mathbb{N}.

(11.7)

Since the proof is similar, we only highlight the main
differences. First, Lemma
9.8
only uses (E1), (E2)
and (E4), so that there exist
n_{6}\geq 1
and
\zeta_{1}>0
such that, for all
x\in K
and all
n\geq n_{6}
,

\delta_{x}P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{K}\geq\zeta_{1}\delta_{x}P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}.

Hence, for all
x\in K
and all
N\geq n_{0}+n_{6}
, using (
11.6
),

\displaystyle\delta_{x}P_{N}\mathbbm{1}_{L}

\displaystyle\geq\gamma_{2}^{n_{0}t_{2}}\,\delta_{x}P_{N-n_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{K}\geq\zeta_{1}\gamma_{2}^{n_{0}t_{2}}\,\delta_{x}P_{N-n_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{E}\geq\zeta_{1}\gamma_{2}^{n_{0}t_{2}}\,\delta_{x}P_{N}\mathbbm{1}_{E}.

Hence,

\inf_{N\geq n_{0}+n_{6}}\inf_{x\in K}\mathbb{P}_{x}(X_{Nt_{2}}\in L\mid Nt_{2}<\tau_{\partial})>0.

(11.8)

Third, it follows from (F2) that, for all
x\in E\setminus L
,

\mathbb{P}_{x}(nt_{2}<\tau_{L}\wedge\tau_{\partial})\leq\gamma_{1}^{nt_{2}}\psi_{1}(x)=\theta_{1}^{n}\varphi_{1}(x).

(11.9)

and from (E2) that, for all
x\in E
,

\mathbb{P}_{x}(nt_{2}<\tau_{\partial})\geq\gamma_{2}^{nt_{2}}\varphi_{2}(x).

(11.10)

Therefore, following the same lines as in (
9.9
) (replacing
K
with
L
), we deduce
from (
11.9
) and (
11.10
) that, for all
x\in E

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}(nt_{2}<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\leq\theta_{1}^{n}\varphi_{1}(x)+c_{3}\int_{0}^{nt_{2}}\inf_{y\in L}\mathbb{P}_{y}\left(\left(n-\lceil s/t_{2}\rceil\right)t_{2}<\tau_{\partial}\right)\,\mathbb{P}_{x}(\tau_{L}\wedge\tau_{\partial}\in\mathrm{d}s)

\displaystyle\leq C\inf_{z\in L}\mathbb{P}_{z}(nt_{2}<\tau_{\partial})\varphi_{1}(x)+\frac{c_{3}\gamma_{2}^{-t_{2}}}{c}\inf_{z\in L}\mathbb{P}_{z}(nt_{2}<\tau_{\partial})\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\gamma_{2}^{-\tau_{L}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}\right),

which entails (
11.7
), where we used in the second inequality the fact that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}(nt_{2}<\tau_{\partial})\geq c\gamma_{2}^{kt_{2}}\inf_{y\in L}\mathbb{P}_{y}\left((n-k)t_{2}<\tau_{\partial}\right),\quad\forall x\in L,

which is deduced from (
11.8
) exactly as in Lemma
9.9
.

Step 4. Proof of (E3).

Using (
11.7
) and the fact that
\sup_{x\in K}\varphi_{1}(x)<+\infty
, we deduce that there exists a constant
C>0
such
that, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in K}\mathbb{P}_{x}(nt_{2}<\tau_{\partial})\leq C\inf_{y\in L}\mathbb{P}_{y}(nt_{2}<\tau_{\partial}).

Moreover, using the Markov property at time
n_{0}t_{2}
and (
11.6
), we have that, for all
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in K}\mathbb{P}_{x}(t<\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{x\in K}\mathbb{P}_{x}(t+n_{0}t_{2}<\tau_{\partial})\geq\gamma_{2}^{n_{0}t_{2}}\inf_{y\in L}\mathbb{P}_{y}(t<\tau_{\partial}).

These inequalities imply (E3).

11.5.2
Existence of a quasi-stationary distribution for
(X_{t})_{t\in I}

Subsection
11.5.1
and Theorem
2.1
imply that there exists a probability measure
\nu_{QSD}
on
E

such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{QSD}}(X_{nt_{2}}\in\cdot\mid nt_{2}<\tau_{\partial})=\nu_{QSD},\ \forall n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+},

such that
\nu_{QSD}(\varphi_{1})<\infty
and
\nu_{QSD}(\varphi_{2})>0
, which is equivalent to
\nu_{QSD}(L)>0
because of the
quasi-stationarity and the form of
\varphi_{2}
. For all
t\in[0,t_{2}]
, let us define the probability measure
\nu_{t}
on
E
by

\displaystyle\nu_{t}=\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{QSD}}(X_{t}\in\cdot\mid t<\tau_{\partial}).

For all
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, we have, using the Markov property and the fact that
\nu_{QSD}
is a quasi-stationary distribution for
(X_{nt_{2}})_{n\geq 0}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{t}}(X_{nt_{2}}\in\cdot\mid nt_{2}<\tau_{\partial})=\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{QSD}}(\mathbb{P}_{X_{nt_{2}}}(X_{t}\in\cdot\mid t<\tau_{\partial})\mid nt_{2}<\tau_{\partial})=\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{QSD}}(X_{t}\in\cdot\mid t<\tau_{\partial}),

hence
\nu_{t}
is a quasi-stationary distribution for
(P_{n})_{n\geq 0}
. Moreover, the third line of (F2) and the quasi-stationarity
of
\nu_{t}
imply that
\nu_{t}(L)
is positive.

Fix
\rho_{1}\in(\theta_{1}^{1/t_{2}},\gamma_{2})
. It follows from (
11.9
) that there exists a constant
C>0
such that, for all
x\in E
,

\displaystyle\varphi^{\prime}_{1}(x):=\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\rho_{1}^{-\tau_{L}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\,\varphi_{1}(x).

We also have that, for all
x\in E\setminus L
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{t_{2}<\tau_{L}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}\varphi^{\prime}_{1}(X_{t_{2}})\right)

\displaystyle=\rho_{1}^{t_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{t_{2}<\tau_{L}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}\rho_{1}^{-\tau_{L}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\rho_{1}^{t_{2}}\varphi^{\prime}_{1}(x)

(11.11)

and the inequality is trivial for
x\in L
. In addition, for all
t\in[0,t_{2}]
and all
x\in L
,

\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\varphi^{\prime}_{1}(X_{t})\mathbbm{1}_{t<\tau_{\partial}}\right)\leq C\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\psi_{1}(X_{t})\mathbbm{1}_{t<\tau_{\partial}}\right)\leq Cc_{2}
. Hence Condition (F) is
satisfied replacing
\gamma_{1}
with
\rho_{1}
and
\psi_{1}
with
\varphi^{\prime}_{1}
. Therefore, we can apply Step 1 to prove that (E) is
satisfied with
\varphi^{\prime}_{1}
and
\varphi^{\prime}_{2}
where

\varphi^{\prime}_{2}=\frac{\gamma_{2}^{-t_{2}}-1}{\gamma_{2}^{-n^{\prime}_{0}t_{2}}-1}\sum_{k=0}^{n^{\prime}_{0}-1}\gamma_{2}^{-kt_{2}}P_{k}\mathbbm{1}_{L}

for an integer
n^{\prime}_{0}
that can be chosen larger than
n_{0}
. We also deduce as in the beginning of Step 2 that

\nu_{QSD}
is the unique quasi-stationary distribution of
(P_{n})_{n\geq 0}
such that
\nu_{QSD}(\varphi^{\prime}_{1})<\infty
and

\nu_{QSD}(L)>0
.

Moreover, by Markov’s property at time
t
we have for all
x\in E
and
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\varphi^{\prime}_{1}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{t<\tau_{L}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}{\rho_{1}}^{-\tau_{L}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}\right]+\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{t\geq\tau_{L}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}{\rho_{1}}^{-\tau_{L}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq{\rho_{1}}^{-t}\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{t<\tau_{L}\wedge\tau_{\partial}}\varphi^{\prime}_{1}(X_{t})\right]+{\rho_{1}}^{-t}\mathbb{P}_{x}(t\geq\tau_{L}\wedge\tau_{\partial})

\displaystyle\leq{\rho_{1}}^{-t}\left(\mathbb{E}_{x}[\mathbbm{1}_{t<\tau_{\partial}}\varphi^{\prime}_{1}(X_{t})]+1\right)

(11.12)

so that, for all
t\in[0,t_{2}]
,

\displaystyle\nu_{t}(\varphi_{1}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq{\rho_{1}}^{-(t_{2}-t)}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{QSD}}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{t_{2}<\tau_{\partial}}\varphi^{\prime}_{1}(X_{t_{2}})\right)/\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{QSD}}(t<\tau_{\partial})+1\right]

\displaystyle\leq{\rho_{1}}^{-(t_{2}-t)}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{QSD}}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{t_{2}<\tau_{\partial}}\varphi^{\prime}_{1}(X_{t_{2}})\right)/\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{QSD}}(t_{2}<\tau_{\partial})+1\right]

\displaystyle={\rho_{1}}^{-(t_{2}-t)}\left(\nu_{QSD}(\varphi^{\prime}_{1})+1\right)<\infty.

Since we observed that
\nu_{t}(L)>0
, we deduce that
\nu_{t}=\nu_{QSD}
for all
t\in I\cap[0,t_{2}]
.

Using the Markov property, we deduce that
\nu_{t}=\nu_{QSD}
for all
t\in I
and hence that
\nu_{QSD}
is a
quasi-stationary distribution for
(X_{t})_{t\in I}
. Since any quasi-stationary distribution for
(X_{t})_{t\in I}
is
also a quasi-stationary distribution for
(P_{n})_{n\geq 0}
, we deduce that
\nu_{QSD}
is the unique quasi-stationary
distribution for
(X_{t})_{t\in I}
such that
\nu_{QSD}(\varphi_{1})<+\infty
and
\nu_{QSD}(L)>0
.

Let
t\geq t_{2}
be fixed and define
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
0\leq t-kt_{2}<t_{2}
. It follows from the fact that
P_{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{1}\leq\bar{C}\varphi^{\prime}_{1}
and from (
11.12
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x}[\mathbbm{1}_{t<\tau_{\partial}}\varphi^{\prime}_{1}(X_{t})]

\displaystyle\leq\bar{C}^{k}\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{t-kt_{2}<\tau_{\partial}}\varphi^{\prime}_{1}(X_{t-kt_{2}})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\bar{C}^{k}{\rho_{1}}^{-(k+1)t_{2}+t}\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{t_{2}<\tau_{\partial}}\varphi^{\prime}_{1}(X_{t_{2}})+\mathbbm{1}_{t-kt_{2}<\tau_{\partial}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\bar{C}^{k}{\rho_{1}}^{-(k+1)t_{2}+t}\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{t_{2}<\tau_{\partial}}\varphi_{1}(X_{t_{2}})+1\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\bar{C}^{k}{\rho_{1}}^{-(k+1)t_{2}+t}(\theta_{1}+c_{2}+1)\varphi_{1}(x).

(11.13)

Note that a similar inequality may not hold true with
\varphi^{\prime}_{1}
replaced by
\varphi_{1}
under our assumptions.
This explains why we need to introduce
\varphi^{\prime}_{1}
.

Now, let
\mu
be a probability measure such that
\mu(\varphi_{1})<\infty
and
\mu(\varphi_{2})>0
. Then, for all
t\geq n_{0}t_{2}
, it
follows from (
11.6
) that, for all
k\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}_{\mu}(X_{t+kt_{2}}\in L)\geq\mathbb{P}_{\mu}(X_{kt_{2}}\in L)\inf_{y\in L}\mathbb{P}_{y}(X_{t}\in L)\geq\gamma_{2}^{t}\mathbb{P}_{\mu}(X_{kt_{2}}\in L).

Therefore, for all
t\in[n_{0}t_{2},(n_{0}+1)t_{2}]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(\varphi_{2}(X_{t}))

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma_{2}^{-t_{2}}-1}{\gamma_{2}^{-n_{0}t_{2}}-1}\sum_{k=0}^{n_{0}-1}\gamma_{2}^{kt_{2}}\mathbb{P}_{\mu}(X_{t+kt_{2}}\in L)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\gamma_{2}^{-t_{2}}-1}{\gamma_{2}^{-n_{0}t_{2}}-1}\gamma_{2}^{(n_{0}+1)t_{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{n_{0}-1}\gamma_{2}^{kt_{2}}\mathbb{P}_{\mu}(X_{kt_{2}}\in L)=\gamma_{2}^{(n_{0}+1)t_{2}}\mu(\varphi_{2}).

This and inequality (
11.13
) imply that (using that
n^{\prime}_{0}\geq n_{0}
), for all
t\in[n_{0}t_{2},(n_{0}+1)t_{2}]
and for a
constant
C>0
that may change from line to line,

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{t}(\varphi^{\prime}_{1})}{\mu_{t}(\varphi^{\prime}_{2})}\leq C\frac{\mu_{t}(\varphi^{\prime}_{1})}{\mu_{t}(\varphi_{2})}\leq C\frac{\mu(\varphi_{1})}{\mu(\varphi_{2})},

where
\mu_{t}:=\mathbb{P}_{\mu}(X_{t}\in\cdot\mid t<\tau_{\partial})
. It then follows the fact that (E) is satisfied by
(P_{n},n\geq 0)
with the
functions
\varphi^{\prime}_{1}
and
\varphi^{\prime}_{2}
that there exist constants
\alpha<1
and
C>0
such that, for all

t\in[n_{0}t_{2},(n_{0}+1)t_{2}]
,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\mu_{t}P_{n}}{\mu_{t}P_{n}\mathbbm{1}_{E}}-\nu_{QSD}\right\|_{TV}

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha^{n}\,\frac{\mu(\varphi_{1})}{\mu(\varphi_{2})},

Using Markov property, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{P}_{\mu}(X_{nt_{2}+t}\in\cdot\mid nt_{2}+t<\tau_{\partial})-\nu_{QSD}\right\|_{TV}

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha^{n}\,\frac{\mu(\varphi_{1})}{\mu(\varphi_{2})}.

This ends the proof of (
3.4
).

11.5.3
Convergence to
\eta

To finish the proof of Theorem
3.5
, it remains to prove that the convergence (
3.5
) is exponential in

L^{\infty}(\psi^{1/p}_{1})
and that
P_{t}\eta=e^{-\lambda_{0}t}\eta
. Because of
Remark
1
, it is enough to prove this for
p=1
. Since we proved that (E) holds true for the semigroup

(P_{n})_{n\geq 0}
and for the functions
\varphi^{\prime}_{1}
and
\varphi^{\prime}_{2}
, it follows from Theorem
2.2
that there exist
constants
\lambda_{0}\in[0,\log(1/\gamma_{2})]
,
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
C>0
such that, for all
y\in E
,

\displaystyle\left|e^{\lambda_{0}nt_{2}}\mathbb{P}_{y}(nt_{2}<\tau_{\partial})-\eta(y)\right|\leq C\alpha^{n}\varphi^{\prime}_{1}(y).

For any
t\in[t_{2},2t_{2}]
, integrating this inequality with respect to
\mathbb{P}_{x}(X_{t}\in dy;t<\tau_{\partial})
, we deduce
from (
11.13
) that

\displaystyle\left|e^{\lambda_{0}nt_{2}}\mathbb{P}_{x}(nt_{2}+t<\tau_{\partial})-\mathbb{E}_{x}(\eta(X_{t})\mathbbm{1}_{t<\tau_{\partial}})\right|\leq C\alpha^{n}\varphi_{1}(x)

for a constant
C
independent of
t\in[t_{2},2t_{2}]
. Setting
\eta_{t}(x)=\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[e^{\lambda_{0}t}\eta(X_{t})\mathbbm{1}_{t<\tau_{\partial}}\right]
,
we obtain for all
t\in[t_{2},2t_{2}]

\displaystyle\left|e^{\lambda_{0}(nt_{2}+t)}\mathbb{P}_{x}(nt_{2}+t<\tau_{\partial})-\eta_{t}(x)\right|\leq Ce^{2\lambda_{0}t_{2}}\alpha^{n}\varphi_{1}(x).

Proceeding as in (
10.2
), we deduce, letting
n\rightarrow+\infty
, that
P_{1}\eta_{t}=e^{-\lambda_{0}t_{2}}\eta_{t}
. It
then follows from Corollary
2.4
that
\eta_{t}(x)=\eta(x)\nu_{QSD}(\eta_{t})
for all
x\in E
. Since we proved above
that
\nu_{QSD}
is a quasi-stationary distribution with decay parameter
\lambda_{0}
, by definition of
\eta_{t}
,

\nu_{QSD}(\eta_{t})=1
and thus
P_{t}\eta=e^{-\lambda_{0}t}\eta
. This ends the proof of Theorem
3.5
.

11.6
Proof of Lemma
3.6

Proceeding as in (
11.11
) and (
11.12
), we have that, for all
x\in E
and
t\in I
,
